Huge New Nvidia Bug Is Ruining Gaming Hardware, Users Say

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia has not been having a good couple of weeks

Nvidia is in rough shape. As the hardware company scrambles to replace its CEO and co-founder, Jensen Huang, after a series of scandals, it seems the needs of lowly gamers aren’t high on the priority list. Earlier this year, the company ran into controversy over its ugly generative AI-fueled DLSS 5 technology. If you need more evidence that Nvidia is focused on cranking out chips for generative AI rather than supporting its gaming hardware, the company has failed to fix a bug reportedly capable of damaging users’ PCs by writing excessive amounts of data to solid-state drives (SSDs).

The bug was found in the last two driver updates for Nvidia graphics cards — specifically, the 621.14 and 621.24 updates for the Nvidia GeForce Game Ready Driver. The 621.24 update went live on Nov. 12. As first reported by TroubleGrid, the bug causes constant writes (saving data) on a user’s SSD, degrading the hardware while potentially causing stutters and mouse pointer hitches. Specifically, the bug apparently causes NVDisplay.Container.exe to write an excessive amount of information to an Nvidia log file, resulting in as much as 30 GB being written per hour.

While this executable already wrote to log files before the most recent driver update, versions 621.14 and 621.24 can cause dramatically more data to be written. It usually takes years for an SSD to degrade naturally, but the excessive data logged by the Nvidia bug could speed up this process. The bug seems to be causing performance issues for some users as data is written in the background. The easiest fix is to downgrade your graphics card driver to version 621.01, which is the last known patch that didn’t have the problem.

Users have reported the bug on most major PC gaming subreddits, and they’re not happy. “Trying to destroy our computers so we stop hoarding all their RAM,” one user said, referencing a now-infamous quote from OpenAI CEO Sam Altman. “AMD and NVIDIA releasing firmware that prematurely kills SSD’s when SSD’s have become 7x more expensive than they were 18 months ago [thinking emoji],” another wrote, conspiratorially implying that these bugs were deliberately created to damage users’ computers and make them buy expensive new SSDs. “Makes you wonder if it’s AI code incompetence or a concerted effort to kill personal computing,” someone else replied.

As this comment suggests, some have suggested that the bug may be a byproduct of Nvidia’s increasing use of AI-generated code. Earlier this year, Huang said he wanted his developers to use AI chatbots and agents more often to help with programming. In that context, this bug may be a Hanlon’s razor situation rather than a conspiracy: "Never attribute to malice that which is adequately explained by stupidity."

Ugly AI graphics and a 50% performance hit, what's not to love?

Julius Randle on Nets' 'embarrassing' blowout loss to Bulls: 'One of the worst games I've ever been a part of'

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Brooklyn Nets wore red, white and blue alternate uniforms on Wednesday night, throwing it back to the franchise's ABA heyday when they welcomed the Chicago Bulls to Barclays Center. The evening featured an appearance by 10 members of the teams that won ABA championships in 1974 and 1976 — including the legendary "Dr. J," Julius Erving.

"I fully anticipated the Nets to be so fired up having us here, they would blow Chicago out," the Hall of Famer said during an appearance on the YES Network broadcast midway through the second quarter. "But I'm looking at 62-39, so … I was wrong."

The Nets scored Wednesday's first basket, with young guard Nolan Traoré getting a runout dunk after Egor Dëmin picked off a Josh Giddey pass. A minute later, Bulls guard Norman Powell knocked down a 3-pointer to put Chicago on top … and, well, that was all she wrote for Brooklyn.

The Nets never again led, or even tied, the visitors, trailing by as many as 37 points in a truly dismal performance that settled as a 137-107 blowout loss. At home. At the hands of what had been a three-win team, with the NBA's fifth-worst offense and absolute worst defense … and without exciting lottery pick Caleb Wilson.

It was a wretched outing — one that saw the Nets commit 20 turnovers leading to 27 Chicago points, and allow 15 first-half 3-pointers and 76 first-half points, the third straight game in which they conceded 70-plus before intermission. Brooklyn gave up 62 total points in the paint, and sat idly by as eight different Bulls scored in double figures.

In the midst of the depressing showing, courtside cameras caught Brooklyn forward Julius Randle lighting into his teammates on the Nets bench:

After finishing the night with 17 points, four assists and no rebounds in 27 minutes of work in the lopsided loss, Randle — who's soldiered through a 17-win season with the Lakers, the post-Anthony Davis doldrums in New Orleans, and the 21-win end of the David Fizdale era in New York — sounded a similarly dour note about the state of affairs for the 3-11 Nets in his postgame press conference.

"That was embarrassing," Randle said, according to Brian Lewis of the New York Post. "Been in the league for 13 years, and that's one of the worst games I've ever been a part of. And not because of the score … but it's how we did it. That wasn't cool. Nothing about it was cool. We've got to find a way to figure out the root of it or whatever, but that's just unacceptable. It's just a pride, an effort, the competitive spirit — all of that, it was nonexistent."

The Nets entered Wednesday intending to celebrate the high-water mark in franchise history, but wound up closer to a historic nadir. According to Erik Slater of Clutch Points, the last two losses "mark the first time they've given up 135 points in back-to-back games since April 1 [and] 2, 1969."

That's a far cry from where the Nets hoped to be after trading this summer for Randle, a three-time All-Star and two-time All-NBA selection who'd starred for the cross-town New York Knicks before helping the Minnesota Timberwolves make a pair of playoff runs, to pair with incumbent sweet-shooting forward Michael Porter Jr. in the frontcourt, in hopes of restoring a franchise that had the third-worst record in the NBA over the preceding three seasons to respectability.

But despite the presence of the 20-point-scoring veteran forwards and some exciting flashes from young guards Traoré, Dëmin and rookie Mikel Brown Jr., Brooklyn has remained mired in the muck, ranking among the league's worst teams on both ends of the court and looking like a team without much of a sense of who it wants to be, how it wants to play, and what its path to success might look like.

"Very disappointed, just starting with effort," head coach Jordi Fernández said after the game, according to C.J. Holmes of the New York Daily News. "We were not a competitive group. We were not a connected group. We were not a selfless group. I saw selfishness. I saw pointing fingers, and I saw guys that didn't always play hard."

That's not the brand of ball that Fernández has preached since arriving in Brooklyn in 2024. The team's desultory effort, though, suggests that maybe what he's preaching isn't quite getting across.

"I can try to say it in Spanish, because in English, they don't understand," Fernández quipped after the game. "So I'll keep trying and find whatever language works. But yeah, our effort was not good."

Julius Randle didn't mince words when describing the state of affairs for his 3-11 Nets. (Caleb Bowlin/Getty Images)

If the coach's message is falling on deaf ears, then it's up to the players to stem the tide and turn things around. Randle appeared to be searching for a way to do so with his impassioned entreaty on the bench; with another game coming against the Orlando Magic on Friday, he says he and his teammates have to look within for the answers they're seeking.

"I think when you win, you find ways to win. When you lose, you find excuses. Seems like a lot of excuses," Randle said, according to Collin Helwig of NetsDaily. "So, you know, for me, I've got to go back into work. I've got to go back into the film. I've got to figure out — I've got to look myself in the mirror tonight, too, like everybody else, and figure out what I can do better. Not point the finger or anything. Like, I got to figure out as a man [...] that has played this game since a kid and love it, and has given a lot to me, I got to figure out how I can help the team and be better for the team. So, you know, I put it on me."

The Denver Broncos have established slightly more stability to the future of their secondary.

Cornerback Riley Moss is signing a four-year, $67 million extension with the team that drafted him, according to multiple reports. Moss was one of six defensive backs – and one of three members of the secondary at the top of the depth chart – poised to hit free agency after the 2026 season. You Can Now Download and Print GTA 6's Official Leonida Map

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you miss the days of unfolding a paper map before heading into a new GTA game, Rockstar has something for you. The official map of Leonida is now available to download for free, and there's a print-ready version for anyone who wants a physical copy.

Rockstar has unveiled The Goodtime State – Vice City Collection, a special bundle packed with physical merchandise celebrating the game's setting.

Rockstar added the map to its GTA 6 website on November 19, the same day the game launched. You can also download the game's cover art as a poster, so there are actually two things worth grabbing if you've got a printer handy.

Head to the official GTA 6 media page and look for the section labeled 'Leonida Map & Cover Art Poster.' Select 'View Options' to open the downloads. Rockstar has separate choices for the Leonida map and cover art, including printable versions of both.

Pick 'Leonida Map - Printable' if you want to put it on paper, then use the download button. There's a regular map download as well, which is handy if you just want to keep a copy on your phone, tablet, or second monitor while you play.

The map gives you a proper look at the shape of Leonida, from Vice City's dense roads to the waterways, coastlines, and smaller areas further inland. If you've spent the last few years comparing trailer shots and fan-made maps, being able to look at Rockstar's official version is a pretty nice change.

If you want the entire thing rather than a cropped section, choose a matching paper size or ask a print shop to scale it to fit. The print-ready download is the version to use for that.

The other download is the game's cover art poster. Rockstar has made it available in a print-ready format too, so you can hang it up instead of just using it as a desktop wallpaper.

There's a bit of GTA history behind the appeal. Physical copies of GTA 3, Vice City, San Andreas, GTA 4, and GTA 5 all came with paper maps. Even spin-offs like Liberty City Stories, Vice City Stories, and Episodes from Liberty City followed that tradition. You could spend ages looking over the streets and place names before you'd actually explored them in-game.

I've lost the map that came with my copy of GTA: Episodes from Liberty City, and I'm sure some of you have misplaced yours too. Even the maps we've managed to keep aren't necessarily in great shape. Leaving them folded inside a game case for years can damage the paper along the creases. Having an official file you can print again is a pretty good solution to that.

You can obviously save the new map and check it on another screen, but printing it is half the appeal. Having something physical to look at while you explore Leonida feels a lot closer to those older releases.

You can obviously save the new map and check it on another screen, but printing it is half the appeal.

I can already see the Leonida map and GTA 6 cover art decorating plenty of fans' rooms. I'd be surprised if a fair few people didn't end up printing both.

If you'd rather have a professionally printed copy, Rockstar has another option coming. In its November 3 announcement, the company said a double-sided physical poster would be sold through the Rockstar Store shortly after launch.

That version will cost $0.99 on its own, or come free with another Rockstar Store purchase. Rockstar hasn't given an exact date for when it will go on sale, though, so don't confuse that upcoming physical item with the digital files you can download right now.

Boxed copies of GTA 6 are available, but as DualShockers previously reported, they contain download codes rather than discs. Reports on the PS5 edition also confirm that the case includes an artwork poster, not the traditional fold-out map. Whether Rockstar will ever bundle a map with a later boxed release is still unclear.

It would've been even better if Rockstar had confirmed that a physical Leonida map would come with the game, like it did with the older releases. Still, making high-resolution printable versions of both the map and cover art available for free is a good move. And unlike an old folded map, you can always print a fresh copy if yours gets damaged.

There's something nice about having an official paper map beside you while playing GTA, and now anyone who wants one can print it without having to track down a boxed copy.

GTA 6 features a completely rebuilt dynamic wind and atmospheric system designed to bring the tropical world of Leonida to life like never before.

Taylor! Lainey! Ella! Kacey! Megan! Backstage at the CMA Awards

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

“Little Lainey would be losin’ her mind if she knew she was about to be hosting the 60th Annual CMA Awards,” Lainey Wilson said ahead of country music’s biggest night at Nashville’s Bridgestone Arena. Indeed, when Vogue caught up with the chart-topper in a quiet moment leading up to the show, she was incredulous about landing the gig.

“I’m still that little girl from a town of 200 people in Louisiana who just grew up dreamin’ about being a small part of this world of country music,” Wilson said in her signature twang. The feeling of going from the middle of nowhere to the center of the ceremony, and in such a milestone year, is nothing short of surreal, she added. Not only was it the show’s six-decade anniversary and a mega year for the genre, but the night was also set to include a tribute to the late Dolly Parton—and the first CMA performance in 13 years from a certain recently wed crossover superstar.

“I’m just excited to celebrate the ones before, the ones now, and the ones comin’,” Wilson said. “I think the biggest thing for me is being present and being right where my feet are. It’s tryin’ to find that balance.”

On the day of the show, Downtown Nashville was a flurry of activity, fans, hot chicken, and road closures as the capital of country music prepared to step into the national spotlight. The recent FAA ground stop gave some out-of-towners agita, but the delay clouds thankfully parted in time. Music was ringing out from every bar and restaurant on Broadway, most of the time in the form of Langley’s ubiquitous “Choosin’ Texas.” The country queen of 2026 was also nominated for almost every award imaginable. It’s to the point where if Langley miraculously cloned herself, they’d be nominated for Best Duo, too.

“This isn’t a fad: everyone you know can sing along to every word of ‘Choosin’ Texas,’” Brittany Schaffer, the incoming Country Music Association CEO, told Vogue. Just hours earlier, Schaffer bolted out of her hotel at 6 a.m. to help give out the first award of the day live on Good Morning America. (Naturally, “Choosin’ Texas” won for Best Music Video.) Schaffer was also busy contending with the Taylor of it all, as her CMA comeback was a closely held secret.

“A small group of us knew for quite a bit of time in advance of the announcement,” Schaffer revealed with a wink. “Obviously, with this being an anniversary, she is such an important part of country music history, so we certainly started that conversation with her early on. We are just thrilled that she was so excited to join.”

While most major red carpets are hectic affairs, the vibe at Music City Center was decidedly chiller—even when an Oscar de la Renta-clad Swift arrived. A hush fell over the space as phones lifted to capture the moment, even that of the lighting technician. Elsewhere, VIPs tucked into fried green tomato sandwiches and sipped whiskey, seemingly unfazed.

“I had to bring my sister, Leslie,” actor Glen Powell, who’s set to play a country music star in next year’s The Comeback King, told Vogue. “ To share this together…I mean, we’ve been country fans our entire lives. We did these long road trips to the ranch, listening to country music the whole way.” A “starstruck” Leslie chimed in: “Two of our childhood idols, Faith Hill and Tim McGraw, are sitting next to us.”

As the Tennessee sun began to set, a series of Sprinter vans whisked people from the carpet to the arena for a 7 p.m. start time. Backstage, a trophy engraver was waiting to etch the names of future winners into history. Inside the arena, the crowd milled about as a booming voice on the loudspeaker urged, “In respect to Dolly, please take your seats.” It proved to be a subtle teaser, as a tribute featuring Wilson and Vince Gill singing “I Will Always Love You” was about to open the show. Later, Kacey Musgraves and Miranda Lambert lightened the mood with their honky-tonk ode “Horses and Divorces,” from Musgraves’s recent album Middle of Nowhere.

From her dressing room, Musgraves, resplendent in a custom suit by the Los Angeles-based western-wear designer RoseCut, took a breather on the couch. “I think people would be shocked to know how boring my pre-show ritual actually is,” she told Vogue. “It’s pretty chill. I don’t shut myself in a hotel room all day. I try to move and connect with the heartbeat of wherever I’m at.” Alas, a vocal doctor did warn once that she should have “no chocolate, no caffeine, or no booze before a performance. “I’m like, ‘Have you ever been on stage without booze,’” she recalled with a laugh. “So pre-show, the band and I usually do a tequila shot.”

According to Musgraves, a night like tonight is all about reconnecting. “So many of us here spend so much of our time out on the road. It’s really a reunion of sorts, getting to see a lot of people and friends that you haven’t seen in so long.”

One of those friends is Megan Moroney, whom Musgraves was happily sharing a dressing room with. Alongside excitement over her two nominations at the CMAs, Moroney is buzzing about her two recently announced Grammy nods. “It’s been such a crazy year,” Moroney told Vogue as the ceremony blared on a TV in the background. “On the morning of the nominations, my manager came into my bedroom at like 6:30 a.m. and said, ‘We need to turn on the Grammy announcement because I just have a good feeling.’ And I was like, ‘Are you insane?!’”

The show itself played out as expected. Ella Langley cleaned up, making history by winning everything from Entertainer of the Year to Female Vocalist of the Year and Album of the Year, breaking a CMA record for most wins. Even the country stalwarts were impressed. “A highlight for me was hugging Miranda Lambert,” Lady A’s Hillary Scott told Vogue of Langley’s collaborator. “What’s funny is we toured with her and Kenny Chesney twenty years ago, early on in our careers.” Her bandmate, Dave Haywood, added: “Longevity was the goal when we started. So to be here after all of these years is a dream realized for us.”

As the show wrapped up and fans departed the arena, stars grabbed snacks to munch on as they got ready to head back out on the road—including a lollipop-sucking Michael Bublé. “What I love about country music is this,” he mused to Vogue, “when you take away all of the production, all of the lights, and all of the magic tricks, you are left with good souls.”

Ripple’s Brad Garlinghouse Says Crypto Markets Could Rebound After Anthropic Goes Public

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse believes the recent crypto market sell-off stems largely from investors rotating capital into high-profile initial public offerings. The Ripple boss and crypto billionaire anticipates a recovery once Anthropic’s massive listing is fully absorbed.

Speaking to CNBC on November 19th, Garlinghouse drew a direct parallel to SpaceX’s public debut earlier in the year. He explained that when major IPOs generate intense demand, investors often sell liquid assets such as Bitcoin and XRP to free up cash for share subscriptions.

The same pattern, he said, unfolded ahead of Anthropic’s offering. After SpaceX completed its listing, crypto markets staged a notable rebound. Garlinghouse expects a similar bounce this time.

Anthropic, the artificial-intelligence company behind the Claude chatbot family, has priced its IPO at $83 per share, above the previously marketed range of $70 to $78.

The deal ranks among the largest IPOs in history, raising tens of billions of dollars and valuing the company in the trillion-dollar range, and shares are set to begin trading on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

Such blockbuster offerings typically attract enormous institutional and retail interest, temporarily diverting capital from other risk assets. Once the primary allocation is complete and secondary-market trading stabilizes, some of that capital often flows back into crypto and other liquid markets.

Bitcoin had slipped near $62,000 on Wednesday before recovering above $63,000 early Thursday. Garlinghouse framed the dip as a temporary capital-rotation effect rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in digital assets. He also expressed confidence that Anthropic’s shares would trade strongly given the strong demand and pricing above the range.

Looking further ahead, Garlinghouse remained optimistic about blockchain’s long-term role. He predicted that within roughly a decade, blockchain technology will be deeply embedded across global financial infrastructure, much as artificial intelligence has already transformed multiple industries.

His comments highlight how large technology IPOs can create short-term pressure on crypto while potentially reinforcing longer-term narratives around innovation and capital markets.

Rockstar Devs Concerned About Layoffs Despite GTA 6 Success

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

GTA 6 is here, and the open world of Leonida is ready for fans to explore. It’s still early days, but GTA 6 is on track to be one of the biggest games ever. It has tied for the best-reviewed game of all time, already generated $1.8 billion in gross revenue and counting, and broken Twitch’s viewership record in just 30 minutes.

The reviews for Grand Theft Auto 6 are in and it's now tied for the best-reviewed video game of all time, with Nintendo's greatest game matching it.

Amid all that success, you would think that GTA 6 developers are now able to kick back and relax, soaking in the excitement of sharing their work with the world. However, even though Rockstar Games hasn't revealed any layoffs, it remains a worry that looms over those who worked on the game.

In an opinion piece published in The Guardian, Animation Programmer at Rockstar Games, Nestor Uriarte, expressed concerns about job security and the pressures developers face.

He says that “while the highs of a game release like this can be incredible,” workers in the video game industry have also become accustomed to “less welcome side effects.” This covers both sides of development, as Uriarte mentions “intense overtime in the buildup and mass redundancies in the aftermath.”

Rockstar Games is one of many studios in previous years to face allegations of “crunch culture,” essentially requiring developers to work an unhealthy amount of overtime in the lead-up to a game’s launch date.

Uriarte believes that “with a strong union we could make sure there is no return to this system. We have already seen the introduction of some overtime pay, and now we want to keep on pushing to ensure that every hour worked in our industry is an hour paid.”

In the middle of the record-breaking release of GTA 6, Rockstar Games employees in the UK are voting on whether or not they want to unionize. The ballot, which will be voted on by approximately 1,600 staff, will close on November 26, 2026. The results of the vote should be announced shortly after voting closes.

Amid all that success, you would think that GTA 6 developers are now able to kick back and relax

Uriarte says that after the launch of GTA 6, Rockstar Games employees "have had no indication that layoffs are coming, but it’s hard not to worry about what the future holds.” He argues that the state of the industry means nobody is safe, “even my colleagues and I, working on the biggest game of all time, are worried about getting laid off. But the truth is that workers across the sector are helpless to resist the waves of redundancies crashing around us, until we get organised.”

Another threat to game development is the rise of AI. Uriarte claims, “Rockstar has said it is committed to preserving jobs and it has been clear that our games will continue to be made by people.” However, the push to secure union recognition would grant employees more of a say in that future.

Layoffs in the video game industry are commonplace, with Civilization studio, Firaxis Games, being the latest to slash its headcount. Other notable redundancies this year include EA shutting down BioWare, and the mass XBOX layoffs as part of the company’s restructuring process, among many others.

Although no layoffs seem to be on the cards at Rockstar Games, if they were to occur, it would call into question the sustainability of game development if a studio with such a huge release needs to let staff go.

GTA 6 removes the ability solicit, a GTA feature since 2001. Rockstar says the decision supports Jason and Lucia’s story.

How Does La Sportiva’s Newest Approach Shoe Stack Up? I Took It to the High Sierra and French Alps to Find Out.

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Hand cracks are not the ideal terrain for the TX1, but they are just too fun to pass up. (Photo: Anthony Walsh)

There are three things that make an approach shoe “high value:” its price, versatility, and durability. At $159, La Sportiva’s all-new TX1 (replacing the cult-classic TX2) is decently affordable, and was versatile enough to take testers across a range of objectives, including fifth-class ridges in the Sierras and daily crag commutes, and be worn as a slip-on between limit boulder problems. I logged dozens of scrambling pitches in my TX1s and noted no signs of premature wear along the TPU-coated polyester upper or its Vibram MegaGrip outsole.

The breathable upper and shallow dot lugs make the TX1 an excellent shoe for the fair-weather rock climber who dodges rainy forecasts and muddy approaches. Those little lugs provide just enough grip for your average walkoff, but really shine on technical, slabby approaches where surface contact is king. On the mile-long Eagle Lake Buttress Traverse (5.0), the TX1 smeared well across its opening slabs—but I wished for a taller rand while jamming hand-sized cracks.

Fit wise, all testers found the TX1 to be comfortable out of the box, thanks to a stock 4mm Ortholite Hybrid insole and a plush Air Mesh lining. (The TX1 fits a touch wider than the TX2, for those familiar.) One tester, who has a relatively narrow, low-volume foot, loved the fit of the TX1, saying its toebox felt roomy and allowed her toes “to spread and relax naturally.” My wider foot, meanwhile, had me eager to take the TX1 off after an hour-long approach. I wish this shoe came in a slightly wider last.

Europe Spent €16.5 Million Proving Again That Hydrogen Burns in a Turbine

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hydrogen burns. Turbines spin. Their exhaust can make steam. Kawasaki demonstrated hydrogen-fired electricity and heat production at Kobe Port Island in 2018, before Europe funded the first HYFLEXPOWER programme. Six years later, Siemens Energy is still issuing announcements about hydrogen combustion at a single turbine in a French paper mill. European and British taxpayers have supplied roughly €16.5 million for the two research programmes, and the same installation has generated at least 78 identified press appearances. Neither the grants nor the publicity have produced a commercial reason to make hydrogen from electricity and burn it for industrial electricity and steam.

The latest Siemens Energy announcement, dated November 16, 2026, describes an SGT-400 turbine at Smurfit Westrock’s Saillat-sur-Vienne paper mill operating on pure hydrogen through startup, load changes and full-load cogeneration. The equipment can operate under those conditions. Hydrogen combustion and heat recovery were already established. Extending the operating range of one manufacturer’s turbine has not changed hydrogen’s production cost, the losses involved in making it or the available alternatives. No second commercial installation has emerged from the programme.

Siemens’ own March 2024 technical article supplied the most important numbers. The electrolyzer installed at the paper mill produces 16.4 kg of hydrogen per hour. The turbine consumes about 810 kg per hour when producing 10 MW of electricity. Onsite production covers 2.02% of hourly fuel demand. Nearly 50 times that electrolyzer capacity would be needed to supply the turbine continuously at the stated operating point. Later testing used hydrogen brought to the site.

The 2023 campaign accumulated about 90 hours of hydrogen and natural-gas blending tests, including just over four hours on pure hydrogen. Siemens identified limited hydrogen availability as the constraint. The 2026 announcement supplies no cumulative full-load hydrogen operating hours, delivered fuel price or routine cost of electricity and steam. These are the figures an industrial customer needs before buying equipment. Announcing another combustion test avoids the question of who will supply hundreds of kilograms of affordable hydrogen every hour for years.

My complete TFIE Strategy Briefing investigation examines the funding records, Siemens’ published operating figures, the energy balance and an audited chronology of 78 press appearances surrounding the same turbine.

At an illustrative 50 kWh of electricity per kilogram of hydrogen, manufacturing the turbine’s 810 kg of hourly fuel requires approximately 40.5 MW of electricity. The turbine returns about 10 MW of electricity plus usable exhaust heat. Compression, storage and delivery add equipment and expense. The mill needs electricity and process steam; converting electricity into gas and burning it to recover those services introduces losses and costs that direct electrification avoids.

France is an especially poor location for this detour. RTE’s 2025 electricity review reports that 95.2% of French generation was low-carbon, with average generation emissions of 19.6 gCO₂e/kWh. Direct grid power can supply the electrical load. Industrial heat pumps, mechanical vapour recompression, electric boilers and thermal storage can supply process heat and steam in combinations designed around the mill’s operating requirements. The programme should have compared these complete systems before committing public money to more hydrogen turbine retrofits.

A paper mill in Finland is already doing the industrial work. Delfort’s Tervakoski plant has operated a Turboden heat pump and mechanical vapour-recompression installation since January 2026. It supplies about 12 MW of process steam at temperatures up to 170°C, with a reported coefficient of performance around two. It delivers useful steam without a hydrogen-production plant, hydrogen deliveries or a hydrogen-fired turbine. The exact plant integration will vary, but the alternative is operating commercially while HyCoFlex continues issuing test announcements.

The funding was directed at the wrong problem. HYFLEXPOWER received €10.48 million in EU support. HyCoFlex received another €4.44 million. A Siemens presentation identifies approximately €1.56 million more in UK support, bringing the public contribution across the programmes to about €16.48 million. Both programmes were designed around making existing gas-turbine cogeneration equipment compatible with hydrogen. The grants paid for additional turbine operating conditions instead of establishing that hydrogen was a sensible way to provide the plant’s energy.

The programme’s economics were weak before the second award. In October 2023, Siemens presented a scenario requiring electricity below €35/MWh to bring hydrogen fuel costs into parity with natural gas under its 2022 cost assumptions. The HYFLEXPOWER final workshop later examined favourable operating cases with hydrogen substitution and electrolyzer utilization both above 90%. European reporting also identified renewable electricity below €40/MWh as a condition for favourable deployment economics. France’s 2025 wholesale electricity averaged €61.1/MWh. Its negative-price hours were occasional. They could not supply a plant operating more than 90% of the time. These conditions were set out before the research programme ended, yet funding continued for more combustion trials.

Siemens also has a clear commercial interest in preserving turbine markets. Its 2025 presentation explicitly described protecting existing assets and future gas-turbine investments in a decarbonizing world as a benefit of hydrogen compatibility. A turbine supplier is entitled to pursue its products with its own money. The purpose of public industrial-decarbonization funding is to reduce emissions and costs, not to protect combustion-equipment sales from direct-electric competition.

The TFIE media audit counted 19 press appearances in 2020, 13 in the 2022 installation and blending phase, 35 around the 2023 tests and award, four in 2024 and seven through November 16, 2026. Those 78 appearances trace back to a handful of announcements about one installation. They are not 78 independent engineering advances or 78 deployments. The headline count swelled while the operating fleet did not.

Public funders should require equipment-neutral comparisons of delivered industrial energy services before underwriting another hydrogen retrofit. The evidence needed is familiar: a year’s reliable electricity and steam, actual operating cost, lifecycle emissions, fuel contracts, and performance against direct-electric alternatives. HyCoFlex has supplied more hydrogen-combustion publicity without establishing those outcomes. The problem was not that Europe failed to make a turbine burn hydrogen. It was that public money kept flowing after the relevant energy and economic questions already had answers.

The full TFIE Strategy Briefing investigation contains the audited funding and media evidence, the 2% onsite hydrogen-supply calculation and the direct-electric industrial steam comparison. TFIE Strategy provides evidence-based assessments of industrial energy investments and public programmes.

This is technically the worst version of GTA 6 that we'll ever play

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I've been playing GTA 6 through the morning to try to see what the fuss is about, and now I totally get it. After mere hours in the game, I'm already coming to terms with the scale of it; not just the size of the map, but the attention to detail on every object and interaction. But I have to remind myself, this is sure to be the worst version of GTA 6 we'll ever play.

That sounds like an insult, but I mean the exact opposite. If this is where the game is at launch, at its most barebones, running on years-old consoles on their way out, then think of what it will be like at its peak.

I work for PC Gamer, so forgive my snobbery when I say the PC version should be far superior to what we're currently playing on consoles.

A part of that is the possible frame rate bump from running on modern PC hardware. The standard PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series S/X are powered by pretty outdated architectures by today's standards: RDNA 2 and Zen 2. You're looking at the Radeon RX 6000 series and Ryzen 3000 series for PC equivalents.

These chips have recently received support for more modern ML-powered FSR upscaling techniques from AMD, though Rockstar has only seen fit to enable FSR 4 on the Xbox Series S to help the underpowered console along. Sony also has AMD-powered upscaling tech for the PS5 Pro, but the standard PS5 doesn't get a whiff of that.

It's only really the PS5 Pro with a more modern GPU—loosely equivalent to an RX 7800 XT—that might temper expectations for how fast the game may run on a modern gaming PC. Rockstar has said the PlayStation 5 Pro wouldn't be capable of a steady 60 fps even with the more powerful hardware compared to the base model; however, hints at the sluggish CPU as perhaps the weak link. The PS5 Pro runs the same old eight-core CPU as the standard PS5, and if Rockstar's engine can make use of faster, more multithreaded CPUs with lots of cache, we should see a performance bump on PC.

That's not to exclude older gaming PCs. The Xbox Series S can cope with GTA 6, so should many gaming PCs of all levels. Notably so if Rockstar offers a range of upscaling options.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

It's ubiquitous upscaling that should make an even bigger difference to an eventual PC release and performance on all manner of PC. We know the game supports FSR 4.1, as both Sony and Microsoft are already using this as the foundation for their upscaling technologies on console, and Red Dead Redemption 2 offers both FSR and DLSS on PC. Since that game's release, we've seen the advent of frame generation technologies from both AMD and Nvidia, which could further increase how smooth GTA 6 feels on PC.

As a serial settings tweaker, I've been sorely disappointed by the sliders, or lack thereof, available to me in GTA 6 on the base PS5. I want levels to this stuff; an advanced menu for advanced men like me. I want to manage the foliage detail, draw distance, and ray-traced effects. Speaking of which, I'm sure we'll have the option to enable ray-traced shadows, currently exclusive to the PS5 Pro, and crank up the quality of most effects in the game on PC.

The non-RT shadows are one of the few noticeable drawbacks about the standard PS5 version of GTA 6 I'm currently playing. They can pop in and out from reflections, and there is a jagginess to them in places.

Is any game going to be modded more than GTA 6? Our Joshua has been busy modding GTA 5 to try to turn it alchemy-like into GTA 6 for months now, and he never ran out of mods to install. While he may have strayed from his original mission almost immediately, the message is clear: GTA 6 is going to have a seriously busy mod scene.

The game will be all the better for it, too. Not every mod is going to enable a roving band of militant furries (Joshua's words, and no need to mod those into GTA 6) or trap the player in a tiny car; I'm sure GTA 6 will actually be a better game for the efforts of modders. It just takes a PC version and some intrepid people to make it happen.

This one is easy; one of the reasons for GTA 5's long-lasting legacy is the combination of GTA Online and custom jobs via the creator. Neither of which are actually here for GTA 6 yet. So on a very basic level, and you don't have to be a PC gamer to agree on this point, GTA 6 is going to get a lot better for the eventual online mode. And there will be an online mode, right? Even if Rockstar hasn't technically announced one is on the way yet.

On the flipside, if you're constantly getting griefed by a guy that only plays GTA 6 all day and won't let you leave your house in GTA Online, perhaps you could argue the game was better when it was single-player only.

It pains me to admit it, but GTA 6 is not only going to eventually run better on PC. It might be the definitive version in my eyes, but console gamers do have a high-fidelity future ahead of them, too. Both Sony and Microsoft are planning new consoles that seem pretty likely to come out in roughly a year's time—providing the memory crisis doesn't ruin everything—and AMD is set to be providing more powerful silicon for these systems.

We're expecting to see the RDNA 5 architecture make its way into the next-gen consoles, paired with Zen 6 CPU cores of some size and description (these may be heterogeneous). That would put the consoles on a level playing field with PC components likely to launch around the same time (Zen 6 should be available on desktop by then, while perhaps a more limited launch for RDNA 5 by early 2028).

So that whole spiel about how current-gen consoles are years behind our fancy PCs won't get me so far once these new systems come out. I'll have to find a new stick to bash consoles with… er, not enough RGB LEDs? Anyways, the definitive GTA 6 experience on console is still yet to come—that'll be the version that's ready to make the most of these more capable units sometime next year or in 2028.

There are going to be a helluva lot of patches, upgrades, and DLCs to come for GTA 6. Remember, GTA V got a new first-person mode with the then-next-gen PS4 and Xbox One, so there's plenty of opportunity for genuine upgrades down the line.

Though, saying that, by recent PC standards (poor ports, game-breaking bugs, and more), this GTA 6 launch has been relatively issue-free. I mean, I've seen a couple of visual glitches and my colleagues have had some strange men falling out of the sky, but besides those open-world oddities you may expect of a game of this size and stature, it's been pretty smooth sailing so far.

I just hope the PC version can claim a similarly clean sweep for its eventual launch. So far, no word on when that might be.

MN: Maignan and Pavlovic expected to return for Milan this weekend – the latest

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

AC Milan can breathe a sigh of relief, with Mike Maignan and Strahinja Pavlovic expected to be starters this weekend.

It’s been a week of nerves for Milan and Ruben Amorim, with questions about two of their most important players: Pavlovic and Maignan. Both have struggled with injury issues during the international break, so there have been concerns about their chances of starting. However, they seem likely to be on the field on Sunday.

According to MilanNews, there will be two welcome names in the XI this weekend with Maignan and Pavlovic expected to be in the team from the get-go. Both have been hit-and-miss this week, but it seems they’ll both be present at San Siro.

Sankhoun Diawara is also expected to be in the defence at left-back, with Mario Gila and Koni De Winter making up the rest of the back four. Then, in midfield, Luka Modric will start with Adrien Rabiot and Yunus Musah either side of him.

Amorim will hope that Christian Pulisic can play after his three goals in the international break, and he’s currently in the expected XI. Then, Diego Moreira and Goncalo Ramos are expected to partner him.

Delta Air Lines CEO Called Wi-Fi Essential, Now Says Passengers Don’t Choose Airlines For It

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Delta Air Lines CEO Ed Bastian once called connectivity essential to daily life and promised an onboard experience resembling what passengers enjoy on the ground. Now, defending the decision to wait for Amazon Leo rather than install Starlink, he says customers do not choose airlines based on Wi-Fi. I understand the argument he is trying to make, but Delta spent years teaching passengers to expect more from their internet connection. Some of us listened.

Speaking with Fortune Editor-in-Chief Alyson Shontell at the Fortune 500 Innovation Forum on November 16, Bastian defended Delta’s connectivity strategy.

“This is about the total experience, and customers are not choosing airlines based on the Wi-Fi.”

He acknowledged enjoying Starlink himself, explained that Delta and SpaceX could not agree on a business model, and expressed confidence that Amazon could deliver a competitive alternative. Bastian also defended the existing Viasat product, describing it as “pretty darn close” to Starlink.

We can debate his last point, but his broader claim about how customers choose airlines strikes me as far too dismissive, especially considering what he has said before.

When Delta announced its free Wi-Fi initiative in January 2023, Bastian described connectivity in very different terms. Back then, he said:

“At work, at home and everywhere in between, connectivity is essential to daily life, and your journey on Delta should be no different.”

In the accompanying T-Mobile announcement, he added:

“The ability to stay connected while seeing the world is simply foundational.”

I agreed with him. Delta made fast, free Wi-Fi part of its premium proposition. The message was that passengers could expect a more useful and enjoyable flight because they could remain connected.

The airline has continued making that case. Its own explanation of why travelers should choose Delta includes fast, free Wi-Fi among the benefits it promotes.

Now that its primary competitor, United, is rapidly approaching a point in which it will have a superior internet product, the message sounds more like passengers should focus on everything else.

I tend to give people the benefit of the doubt and Bastian is no exception.

Saying connectivity is essential does not necessarily mean passengers will select the airline with the fastest connection. Bastian could reasonably argue that Delta already provides sufficiently good Wi-Fi and that another improvement in speed will not overcome differences in schedules, service, or loyalty.

But saying customers do not choose airlines based on Wi-Fi goes considerably further. Some certainly do. I am one of them. Delta cannot spend years promoting connectivity as part of what makes its product worth choosing, then expect no scrutiny when its CEO downplays connectivity as a reason to choose a competitor.

Perhaps Bastian has data showing that relatively few passengers have changed their booking habits. I would be interested in seeing it. His statement, however, leaves little room for customers whose behavior already contradicts it. He’s essentially inviting them to fly to United instead.

Not that I fly Delta often, but unlike some of my colleagues I’ve had very good experiences with Wi-Fi on Delta.

The Viasat Wi-Fi I have used on both American Airlines and Delta has generally been fine for the work I do. I can work on the blog, handle email, and complete other tasks without needing the fastest connection available.

Starlink or Viasat, I just need my connection to work and I should not have to wonder whether it will (one reason there are many aircraft on United, like the 757-300, that I avoid like the plague). If Viasat lets me accomplish what I need to accomplish, that flight remains productive and I’m a happy passenger. I am not going to pretend otherwise simply because I prefer Starlink.

But Starlink has raised my expectations. It is objectively faster and more responsive and it more closely resembles working from the ground…always. When I have comparable options, that influences my decision. I have already explained how I try to improve my odds of finding Starlink on United Airlines.

That does not mean I would accept any fare or routing to get it, but it means connectivity enters the calculation alongside departure time and price, particularly on a daytime flight. For someone whose work travels with him, several productive hours onboard have immense value…and I cannot relax on a plane if there is work to be done (that miserable United 777 flight from SFO-EWR comes to mind).

Bastian is right that passengers evaluate the total experience, but the internet connection is part of that experience and increasingly a critical part of the experience as passengers expectations evolve.

A good crew and comfortable seat are welcome and in some ways foundational, but so is being able to finish work before landing rather than spending the evening catching up instead of spending it with my family.

Different passengers will assign different values to those things. Someone taking a vacation may be delighted to disconnect. Someone traveling during the workday may be far more concerned about whether the connection will support the tasks waiting on a laptop. I do not expect Bastian to concede that every passenger should leave Delta for United, which is just a stupid argument. But recognizing that connectivity can influence a booking would hardly undermine the rest of Delta’s product…Bastian suddenly sounds defensive and tone deaf.

It would simply acknowledge how some customers use their time onboard…

I have defended the commercial logic behind Delta choosing Amazon Leo. An airline has legitimate reasons to consider control over its passenger interface and the broader business relationship when selecting a technology partner. We should also welcome competition for Starlink. Having another capable provider would be good for airlines and passengers.

But Delta plans to begin installing Leo on an initial 500 aircraft in 2028. That leaves time for competitors to establish new expectations, even if Amazon ultimately delivers an excellent product. As I discussed earlier this week, the failed launch carrying 40 Amazon satellites illustrates the execution risk involved in waiting for a network still being built, even if Delta has multiple back-up options.

Delta may have made the right long-term choice: only time will tell. But that does not mean passengers will ignore what competitors offer in the meantime and I’m fairly comfortable in saying Delta will lose a fair number of customers due to its Wi-Fi disparity with United.

Bastian once described connectivity as essential and foundational. He was right then, making his recent remarks puzzling, even when taken in context. I also concede that the Viasat service on American and Delta has generally worked well for my needs. This is not a case of Starlink being the only usable internet in the sky.

But reliable Wi-Fi now influences which flights I book, and Starlink increasingly gives me another reason to choose one departure over another and specifically, to choose United Airlines over others. Delta helped make connectivity something passengers expect. It should not be surprised when passengers begin choosing airlines based on who delivers it best.

Does Wi-Fi influence which airline or flight you book?

Working both in the aviation industry and as a travel consultant, Matthew has been featured in major media outlets around the world and uses his Live and Let's Fly blog to share the latest news in the airline industry, commentary on frequent flyer programs, and detailed reports of his worldwide travel.

Il Napoli come l'Inter di Inzaghi: dopo la figuraccia dello scorso 7 novembre solo conferenze obbligatorie

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Nei giorni scorsi aveva fatto discutere la scelta del Napoli di annullare all’improvviso e con i giornalisti già presenti in sala stampa la conferenza di Massimiliano Allegri alla vigilia del match contro la Lazio. Erano le 15.15 dello scorso 7 novembre, col tecnico livornese che nel giro di pochi minuti avrebbe dovuto incontrare i giornalisti alla viglia del match. Niente da fare invece, con l’annullamento voluto dal presidente De Laurentiis per isolare la squadra in un momento delicato come è quello del ritiro. Anche perché Allegri difficilmente ama sottrarsi al confronto con la stampa, storicamente.

In queste ore il Napoli, onde evitare figuracce come quella e per non riproporre una mancanza di rispetto verso i cronisti che abitualmente seguono le vicende azzurre, ha messo le mani avanti e fatto sapere che i tesserati azzurri parleranno in conferenza stampa solo in occasione delle gare di Champions League, come da obblighi UEFA, e nei post partita di Serie A. Le conferenze stampa della vigilia, invece, saranno fatte solo in determinati momenti a totale discrezione del club.

Una scelta che da una parte eviterà fastidiosi disguidi come quello dello scorso 7 novembre, in fondo anche altre società come l’Inter nell’era Simone Inzaghi avevano adottato questa strategia comunicativa. Dall’altra però evidenzia la scarsa voglia di far conoscere stati d’animo e dinamiche interne al gruppo squadra e alla società, il tutto penalizzando quei tifosi che aspettano di capire concetti e notizie della settimana. Al netto dell’Inter inzaghiana sono poche le squadre che adottano certi modi di fare comunicazione. Una situazione probabilmente viziata dai tanti bassi e pochi alti di questo avvio di stagione, che sarà quindi valutata di volta in volta dall'ufficio stampa del Napoli e, ovviamente, dal presidente Aurelio De Laurentiis.

Going Up: Sharp Uptick in Rates After Four Weeks of Declines

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Estate tax,Model house with Percentage symbol icon on green background,Business investment and Property tax concept

Mortgage rates moved higher this week, but experts say even against the backdrop of potential Fed rate increases and record-low home sales, there are some advantages in the housing market for buyers who remain active.

The latest Freddie Mac Primary Mortgage Market Survey® released Thursday shows the average 30-year fixed-rate mortgage rate at 7.28%, up from 7.11% last week. The 30-year FRM is still up year-over-year from 6.26% last year. The 15-year FRM averaged 6.66%, up from last week’s 6.45%. A year ago at this time, the 15-year FRM averaged 5.54%.

At press time, the average rate was up to 7.46% Thursday afternoon, according to Mortgage News Daily.

Bright MLS Chief Economist Lisa Sturtevant broke down the numbers for buyers, explaining at current interest rates, and assuming a 10% down payment and average property tax and home insurance costs, the monthly payment on the median-priced home in the U.S. is $3,100. A homebuyer needs an income of over $130,000 to qualify to purchase the median priced home. The median household income in the U.S. is currently about $90,000.  Renters have lower incomes, with a median of about $60,000, she said.

“Last year at this time, when mortgage rates were 100 basis points lower, the median mortgage payment was $250 lower. The difference is enough to price some prospective homebuyers out of the market,” Sturtevant added. “There are other would-be homebuyers who could take on this higher rate but instead are looking ahead to 2027 hoping for more favorable conditions.”

She also said the desire for homeownership remains strong, particularly among young adults, according to recent surveys. “We should expect buyers to return to the market in the coming years; however, higher rates are forcing many young adults to delay, which means they are also delaying the wealth accumulation that comes through homeownership.”

Realtor.com Senior Economist Anthony Smith said hot inflation data and the persistence of the conflict in Iran is placing upward pressure on bond yields and borrowing costs.

“The October CPI report came in above expectations on both the headline and core measures, cementing market expectations for a December rate hike and giving the 10-year little room to fall.

“The debate has shifted from whether the Fed hikes in December to how aggressively it moves,” he added, noting that backdrop is tough for mortgage rates heading into what is already a seasonally slow stretch for the housing market.

“Pending home sales fell to a record low in October, and existing home sales hit their lowest level since 2010, as the surge in rates through mid-October took its toll on buyers and sellers alike. Builder confidence has also fallen to a 14-year low, and permit activity dropped to its lowest level since the early days of the pandemic, signaling that the supply pipeline will face continued pressure in the months ahead,” Smith said.

But for buyers who remain active, Smith noted conditions offer some real advantages.

“With fewer shoppers in the market, competition has thinned, sellers have shown more flexibility on price, and active inventory continues to rise even as new listings have pulled back. The challenge is that until inflation cools enough to put mortgage rates on a durable downward path, affordability will remain the dominant constraint on the market.”

Added benefits. Greater flexibility. Solutions that simplify homeownership. See how American Home Shield can help you stand out and deliver more. See how.

Google signs deal with Blue Origin to send satellite data centers into orbit

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This is part of Project Suncatcher, an experiment aimed at running AI systems in space.

Google has partnered up with Jeff Bezos' Blue Origin to deploy small satellite data centers into space, as part of its Project Suncatcher project. Each of these satellites will be equipped with "roughly the capacity of a data center rack" and will enter into a sun-synchronous orbit via heavy-lift New Glenn rockets.

The rocket will carry several Google tensor processing units, the company's proprietary AI chips, along with data center servers. The first launch isn't scheduled until early 2029, so we have a couple of years to see if this whole project even works.

Blue Origin and Google have entered into a launch services agreement for Project Suncatcher, Google's research moonshot exploring how an interconnected network of solar-powered satellites equipped with its AI chips, Tensor Processing Units (TPUs), could scale machine learning... pic.twitter.com/dUWuHR4Pla

— Blue Origin (@blueorigin) November 19, 2026

This is an experiment that Google calls a "long-term moonshot" to see if it's possible to put AI infrastructure in space by "tapping into the abundant solar power in orbit." The benefits of this would be massive. Data centers here on Earth are notorious hogs, with their snouts all over electricity grids, land and cooling systems.

The hope is that the harsh vacuum of the cosmos will be a better fit for the technology. After all, in space nobody can hear you make endless industrial noises and high-pitched whirs all day every day. This is a very real situation for folks living near data centers.

Google is far from the only company trying to kick the data center can straight into outer space. Elon Musk's SpaceX has been experimenting with the idea, though it may involve putting a gigantic catapult on the moon (really.) There are also some startups fiddling with the technology, like Starcloud.

Can this even work? We don't know yet, though there are some serious hurdles to overcome. Google did deploy its first AI server satellite back in October, but it was sent into low-Earth orbit and can only operate for 15 minutes at a time. Scaling up the affiliated cooling systems looks to be incredibly difficult.

That's because standard heat dissipation doesn't work in space, so it has to be released through pipes and panels rather than conventional cooling systems. There's also the matter of radiation. It's everywhere and causes something called "bit flip." This essentially flips the binary code from a one to a zero, or vice versa. Obviously, this would be a major issue for binary-coded systems.

Did I mention the actual launches? It's extremely expensive to send stuff to space. Just one Falcon 9 launch costs around $74 million and the maximum payload can barely cover the hardware for a small server room, let alone a hyperscale data center.

"Expanding from one satellite to a vast network of them that operate like a giant data center will take years and enormous funds," Brandon Lucia, a professor of electrical and computer engineering at Carnegie Mellon University, told The New York Times. "If you do this on a large scale, there are additional engineering problems."

Finally, there's a risk to us humans here on Earth. Scientists have been considering a theoretical scenario called Kessler syndrome for decades. This is when the density of objects in low-orbit gets so high and collisions become so commonplace that it creates a chain reaction of debris. We would be on the receiving end of that debris.

This theory was posited back when there were just under 2,000 items circling a low-Earth orbit. That number has since risen to over 47,000 and Elon Musk recently floated an idea to add millions of new satellites into the mix.

Meta’s AI agent Muse is mixing up conversations and exposing private data, report says

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Meta employees have raised concerns that the company’s AI agent Muse has been mixing up conversations across different accounts and exposing private user data, according to a new Reuters report that looked at internal company posts.

In some of these instances, employees said Muse surfaced messages about childcare requests they never made, career advice they never asked for, product orders they never placed, and bank account balances that didn’t belong to them.

One employee told colleagues that Muse had randomly sent an extra message in a chat about weather and tide information, saying: “BTW, two bank updates, your Capital One balance is $2722.58, your Charles Schwab balance is $5899.57.”

In another example, an employee using the AI agent on her personal phone received a reminder from Muse to respond to a message from her sister on Signal, the popular end-to-end encrypted messaging app.

“This seems like an extreme privacy violation. I definitely did not grant it access to other apps, or recall being asked about this,” the employee said in her post, according to the report.

Launched in September, Muse is one of the fastest-growing apps on record, with more than 10 million downloads.

Meta did not immediately respond to TechCrunch’s request for comment.

Transformers Confirms 2027 Release With Optimus Prime And Megatron Returning

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Transformers franchise is in the midst of one of its most exciting new eras, and 2027 is bringing fans another major release to look forward to. With Optimus Prime and Megatron at the center of an escalating war between the Autobots and Decepticons across both Earth and Cybertron, this upcoming release delivers a pivotal chapter in the franchise's ongoing saga that fans won't want to miss.

If you haven’t been keeping up with all things Transformers, then you may have missed superstars Robert Kirkman (Invincible, The Walking Dead) and Dan Mora (Justice League Unlimited, Superman) kicking off a new era for the franchise within Image Comics’ ongoing Transformers series, part of the larger Energon Universe saga.

Previously, the series was spearheaded by the A-list trio of writer Daniel Warren Johnson, artist Jorge Corona, and colorist Mike Spicer, who were responsible for Transformers #1–24. This Eisner Award-winning run was nothing short of exceptional and became a fan favorite, meaning there were some exceptionally large and intimidating shoes to fill for whichever creative team took over.

However, Kirkman, Mora, Corona, Jason Howard, and Spicer have continued to deliver with the series, earning a largely positive reception from readers, as reflected in the ratings for their currently available Transformers #25–38.

Mighty Morphin Power Rangers fans have an epic new release to look forward to in 2027, as the classic team they fell in love with returns.

Now, collectors and those who simply prefer trade releases so they can binge their favorite stories in one go have a new release to look forward to on April 29, 2027. Image Comics’ upcoming Transformers Deluxe Edition Book 3 HC will collect the first chapter of this Robert Kirkman-led era, making it an ideal opportunity for fans to experience this exciting new direction in one volume.

This first chapter of Transformers’ new era will be collected in Transformers Deluxe Edition Book 3 HC, a 280-page mega-release that will be available for $49.99 upon its April 2027 release. This hardcover will collect Transformers #25–36, along with a story from Energon Universe Special 2026. This format is perfect for collectors and those who simply prefer to enjoy their stories in a comprehensive collected edition rather than following individual issue releases.

While all 12 issues are spearheaded by legendary writer Robert Kirkman, fans can expect contributions from various artistic talents, including Dan Mora (Superman), Jason Howard (Astounding Wolf-Man), Jorge Corona (Lobo), and Ludo Lullabi (Ghost Pepper). However, colorist Mike Spicer remains a consistent creative presence throughout the collection.

For those who haven’t been following along with the individual issue releases, Transformers #25–36 explores a narrative in which the Autobots and Decepticons embark on the most surprising new chapter of their war across Earth and Cybertron. Those who favor Optimus Prime and Megatron will also be pleased to learn that much of the narrative revolves around these two iconic leaders.

These issues notably explore Optimus Prime and Elita One clashing over the future of the Autobots, a conflict that will have consequences capable of dividing their forces. New allies are also introduced, including Arcee Magnus. On the Decepticon front, fans can expect secrets to be revealed as Megatron experiences a vision that will change the course of his life and, more consequentially, his leadership of the Decepticons.

So, if you prefer to indulge in your Transformers stories with a gorgeous, bookshelf-worthy collection, you'll want to keep this release on your radar.

Franchise creator Andrzej Sapkowski’s Sword of Destiny short-story collection is getting a new series adaptation set to arrive this summer.

Transformers Deluxe Edition Book 3 HC from Image Comics will be available on April 28, 2027!

Trump, Putin and Xi release 'peace declaration' after historic meeting

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: What did Trump, Xi and Putin get out of their historic meeting?

US President Donald Trump and his Russian and Chinese counterparts Vladimir Putin and Xi Jinping have issued a "joint declaration of peace" following historic trilateral talks.

The three leaders met for 90 minutes in China's south-eastern city of Shenzhen, on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) summit.

While the presidents have previously had one-on-one meetings, these are their first trilateral talks.

In the declaration, they said "nuclear war cannot be won and must never be fought" and that the countries planned to increase the production and supply of petroleum. They also said they were "committed to reform the UN" to respond more effectively to global challenges.

Thursday's statement also flagged the issue of immunity enjoyed by state officials and said "they agreed that the International Criminal Court and other similar institutions must not abuse their jurisdiction".

Trump praised the trilateral meeting calling it "a great and highly consequential trip", in a social media post while flying back to Washington on Air Force One.

In the same post, the president announced he would host a news conference to talk about the trip on Friday at the White House.

While in China, Trump met Putin ahead of the trilateral, in their first meeting since August 2025, where the US president greeted his Russian counterpart as his "friend".

He also spoke with Xi in a separate conversation, describing the pair's relationship as "very special".

Beyond the warmth and shared niceties, however, the three men had different goals and motivations for the sit-down.

This is a remarkable diplomatic moment for China's Xi - but it comes with pitfalls

The opening remarks of the trilateral gave some indication about what was to be discussed during the closed-door meeting.

Before giving the floor to his fellow leaders, Xi said that "positive interaction among major powers is not a matter of choice, but a mandatory requirement".

Putin followed this by highlighting a shared interest in ending the Ukraine war and said the conversation would also touch on food and energy security.

China has officially remained neutral since Putin launched a full-scale invasion of Ukraine in 2022, rejecting accusations from Kyiv and its allies that it has been tacitly supporting Moscow, including by exporting dual-use technology used by the Russian military.

Beijing has also repeatedly called for negotiations and a political settlement while avoiding condemnation of Russia's aggression.

Trump greets 'my friend' Putin ahead of meeting with Xi

Meanwhile, Trump closed by saying the presidents of three of the world's largest countries have a "special responsibility" to maintain productive dialogue.

"Too often past leaders have avoided talking to nations with whom they had disagreements," he said, adding: "We feel the opposite."

Trump referenced the three countries' nuclear capabilities and said they are "uniquely positioned to discuss how to reduce the number of nuclear weapons on Earth".

It is "vital" that they co-operate to "combat the threat of nuclear proliferation in other countries around the world", he said, adding: "That's what we're doing in Iran."

"We're not going to let Iran have a nuclear weapon".

Trump then turned to Ukraine and said he and Putin have had a "long discussion" on the conflict there already.

He added that Putin "and I hope the other side, would like to see an end to that war".

Putin addressed the media following the trilateral meeting and described it as an "important chapter in the history of diplomacy".

He said Ukraine and Europe were not yet ready to make compromises to resolve the conflict in Ukraine - but he said Russia had invited EU representatives to Moscow to better understand Europe's position.

As the talks were under way in China, and even as Putin was holding his press conference claiming to be ready for peace, Ukraine's capital Kyiv was under attack from Russian drones.

Putin has repeatedly rejected all ceasefire offers from Ukraine, insisting on his maximalist demands which Kyiv and its European allies say are tantamount to de facto capitulation of Ukraine.

In Shenzhen, the Kremlin leader also vowed a "far more painful" retaliation in response to Ukrainian attacks on the bridge connecting Russia to Ukraine's occupied peninsula of Crimea. A missile and drone attack on Tuesday destroyed its central road section.

There was no mention from Putin of almost five years of Russian attacks on power plants, bridges and residential buildings in Ukraine.

The Russian president also directly addressed comparisons between his meeting with Trump and Xi and the 1945 Yalta Conference, where the leaders of the Soviet Union, Britain and the US met towards the end of World War Two.

"Nobody divided up the world at Yalta. It simply codified the realities that had emerged after WW2.

"And when it comes to today's conflicts, the realities on the ground will have to be accepted."

The three presidents spoke behind closed doors at the Kylin Villa, the exclusive resort in Shenzhen

The Kremlin released a video showing Putin leaving the summit venue and saying goodbye to Xi.

"Thank you for organising this," Putin said. "I don't think anyone even expected everything to go so well."

Meanwhile, China's president described his meeting with Putin and Trump as "positive, constructive, and highly productive", according to the country's state broadcaster CCTV.

It added that Xi recalled "the history of fighting side-by-side during WW2", as the three leaders emphasised the importance of "cherishing the hard-won peace".

Thursday's trilateral meeting was reportedly the idea of Putin.

Some Russian newspapers called it a "diplomatic victory" as after Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine many Western leaders were trying to isolate him.

The summit also marked the first time since Russia's war in Ukraine began that Putin took part in an international gathering attended by leaders from countries including Canada, Japan and Australia.

Russian 'nuclear briefcase' spotted at APEC summit

US lawmakers responded with frustration to Trump's warmth towards America's geopolitical adversaries.

"I was encouraged by some of the commitments that came out of the President's meetings in Asia, particularly the recognition that nuclear war must never be fought," Republican Senator John Curtis said in an emailed statement to the BBC.

But Curtis also said he was concerned about Trump's approach to the foreign leaders, adding that "Vladimir Putin and Xi Jinping are not our friends".

"We should not treat them as though they share our values or interests," Curtis, who sits on the Senate foreign relations committee, added.

"Doing so risks undermining our national security, weakening trust with our allies, and sending the wrong message about where America stands."

Jeanne Shaheen, a ranking Democratic member on the same committee, chose harsher words in her comment.

"President Trump's 'friend' is a murderous war criminal," she wrote on X.

With additional reporting from Brandon Drenon

Docker patches Java4Shell and the NGINX HTTP/2 with Docker Hardened Images

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

OpenJDK and Oracle disclosed Java4Shell (CVE-2026-114938) on November 17, and NGINX released fixes for two HTTP/2 flaws the next day. Docker began publishing fixed images for both in the Docker Hardened Images (DHI) catalog starting that same day.

What we know about Java4Shell is that it’s a memory-corruption bug in the Java runtime that can let an attacker with no credentials run code inside a Java application. Many container images contain a Java runtime, including application images that bundle their own, so it affects teams well beyond the ones that write Java.

Docker Hardened Images are the trusted foundation your applications should run on, because a flaw in the runtime inside an image is fixed only when that image is rebuilt, and Docker rebuilds its hardened images as soon as upstream fixes become available. Fixed images for the Amazon Corretto, SapMachine and Azul Zulu runtimes and for NGINX are in the catalog today, with Eclipse Temurin following as soon as Adoptium completes its fixed builds.

To decode Base64 quickly, the HotSpot JVM uses a hand-written routine built on AVX-512 instructions. That routine miscalculates a length when the input ends in unexpected padding and writes up to 62 bytes past the end of a buffer on the Java heap. Calif, the researchers who found it, turned that into heap disclosure and remote code execution.

They demonstrated code execution before authentication against default installations of Keycloak, Jira, Confluence, Artifactory, Jenkins and PingFederate, and after authentication against Kafka, Solr, Elasticsearch, Gerrit and Spinnaker. They note there is no universal exploit: each application needs its own, and they have not published those. As of November 18 there are no public reports of exploitation in the wild.

Oracle rates the issue 8.1 (High) under CVSS 3.1. The researchers rate it 9.2 (Critical) under CVSS 4.0. CISA has issued an alert urging immediate patching.

To check a host’s CPU, run this on the host or Kubernetes node. Hardened runtime images have no shell, so it will not run inside the container.

grep -m1 ‘^flags’ /proc/cpuinfo | tr ‘ ‘ ‘\n’ | grep -E ‘^(avx512f|avx512bw|avx512vbmi|bmi2)$’

The host is affected if all four flags are printed.

Two other ways to check, for when a shell on the node is hard to get.

Ask the JVM. This works in hardened images:

docker run –rm –entrypoint java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version | grep -w UseAVX

UseAVX = 3 means the JVM is using AVX-512 on that host. Treat the host as affected until the full check says otherwise. A value of 0 to 2 means AVX-512 is not in use. On ARM64 nothing is printed. The answer applies only to the host the command ran on.

Ask Kubernetes. kubectl get nodes -L kubernetes.io/arch,node.kubernetes.io/instance-type shows the architecture and instance type of every node. If you run Node Feature Discovery, nodes with the instruction set carry the label feature.node.kubernetes.io/cpu-cpuid.AVX512VBMI=true.

Check build runners and CI workers as well. They run Java, and they talk to the network.
