docker run –rm –entrypoint java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version | grep -w UseAVX

UseAVX = 3 means the JVM is using AVX-512 on that host. Treat the host as affected until the full check says otherwise. A value of 0 to 2 means AVX-512 is not in use. On ARM64 nothing is printed. The answer applies only to the host the command ran on.

Ask Kubernetes. kubectl get nodes -L kubernetes.io/arch,node.kubernetes.io/instance-type shows the architecture and instance type of every node. If you run Node Feature Discovery, nodes with the instruction set carry the label feature.node.kubernetes.io/cpu-cpuid.AVX512VBMI=true.

Check build runners and CI workers as well. They run Java, and they talk to the network.

Fixed. The published images report 17.0.21.1.2, 21.0.13.1.2, 25.0.5.1.2 and 27.0.1.1.4.

Fixed. The published images report 21.0.13.1+2, 25.0.5.1+2 and 27.0.1.1+4.

Fixed. The published Zulu images we checked report 17.0.21.1+2, 21.0.13.1+2, 25.0.5.1+2 and 27.0.1.1+4.

Waiting for Adoptium to complete its release. On November 18 Adoptium had published 27.0.1.1+4 for x86-64 Linux only. 17.0.21.1, 21.0.13.1 and 25.0.5.1 were still in testing. Adoptium tracks progress in adoptium/temurin#326. Our published images still report 17.0.21+9, 21.0.13+9, 25.0.5+8 and 27.0.1.0.2+1, which are vulnerable.

Java 26 reached end of support in September 2026, so no fix is expected from any vendor, and it is not among the fixed lines in the OpenJDK advisory. If you run it, treat it as affected and keep the flag set. We recommend moving to Java 25 or updating to Java 27. If you cannot migrate yet, ask us whether Extended Lifecycle Support can cover your version. It is the Docker Hardened Images Enterprise add-on for software past its upstream end of life.

Not affected by this CVE and no action is needed for it. The same November update fixes other issues in these lines, so update when new images arrive.

Application images that bundle Java, such as Keycloak, Jenkins, Kafka, Elasticsearch and Solr

They follow the bundled runtime, which for many of them is Temurin, and they are rebuilt once the runtime they use is fixed. Until then, set the flag yourself.

Set the mitigation flag yourself on every JVM that is still waiting for a fixed runtime, and check what each image already sets before you do. Treat the flag as a stopgap, because a JVM is protected only if the flag reaches it when it starts, and a deployment setting or a start script can override it or drop it. A fixed runtime is the only thing that closes the issue, so move to the fixed image as soon as it is published.

The CPU check and the mitigation flag in the steps below apply to any image that runs a HotSpot-based OpenJDK build. The version strings in this post come from Docker Hardened Images, so for an image from another source, confirm the fixed build in that source’s advisory.

The fixed build can only come from whoever builds your image, because the Java runtime and the system packages your application runs on come from the image underneath it. That dependency is why a hardened image is a necessary step toward a trusted software supply chain. With Docker Hardened Images, tracking each vendor’s fix and rebuilding on it is work Docker does for you. The images are minimal and secure by default, and they are built to serve as a trusted and verifiable upstream for your builds. You can move a service onto one by following the migration guide.

A container often runs more than one JVM, for example when a build tool forks a JVM for tests, an application server starts helper processes or a CI agent starts a JVM for every build step. The flag protects only the JVMs that receive it, and the variable most people reach for first is JAVA_OPTS, which Java does not read at all.

The JVM itself, however it is started. Includes javac, keytool and other JDK tools.

A Java 8 JVM that sees it refuses to start. Some DHI FIPS images already set this variable.

Not read by javac, keytool or other JDK tools. DHI FIPS images set it. Java 8 ignores it.

JAVA_OPTS, JENKINS_JAVA_OPTS, MAVEN_OPTS, ES_JAVA_OPTS and similar

A start script, which copies the value onto one java command line

Each script has its own rules. If nothing in the image reads the variable, it does nothing.

Five rules follow from that table. We tested each on DHI images.

JAVA_OPTS only works where a script reads it. With JAVA_OPTS=”-XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics” set and a plain java command, the intrinsic stays on. No warning is printed.

A flag on the command line stops at that process. We started a JVM with the flag on its command line and had it start a second JVM. The child ran with the intrinsic on. With JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS or JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS in the environment, the child ran with it off. A child started with a cleared environment gets nothing, whichever variable you use.

Check what the image already sets before you set anything. In Docker and Kubernetes, your value replaces the image’s value. It is not appended.

docker inspect –format ‘{{range .Config.Env}}{{println .}}{{end}}’ \  | grep -E ‘^(JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS|JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS)=’

Our FIPS images use these variables to configure the FIPS provider. dhi.io/eclipse-temurin:21-fips sets both. When we replaced its JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS with only the flag, the JVM started normally, but the org.bouncycastle.fips.approved_only property was gone. Nothing warned us. If the command prints a value, copy it and add the flag at the end:

env:  – name: JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS    value: “ -XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics”

Not every FIPS image uses the same variables, so run the check for each image.

Keep JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS away from Java 8. Java 8 is not affected and does not know the flag:

Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS: -XX:-UseBASE64IntrinsicsUnrecognized VM option ‘UseBASE64Intrinsics’Error: Could not create the Java Virtual Machine.

Adding -XX:+IgnoreUnrecognizedVMOptions to the variable does not help on Java 8. Where Java 8 shares an environment with a newer Java, either scope the variable to the containers that need it or use JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, which Java 8 ignores. Java 11 accepts the flag, so it is harmless there.

Verify the process, not the environment. Running java -XX:+PrintFlagsFinal -version inside a container starts a new JVM. It tells you what the environment gives a new JVM, not what the JVM already serving traffic was started with. For the running process, use the startup log line. JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS prints one too, starting with NOTE: Picked up. In an image that includes the JDK you can also ask the process directly:

jcmd VM.flags | tr ‘ ‘ ‘\n’ | grep UseBASE64Intrinsics

The answer must be -XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics. If the flag reached the JVM through an environment variable, it will not show in the process’s command line, so ps is not a reliable check.

Some applications discard these variables on purpose. Elasticsearch’s start script, for example, ignores JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS and expects ES_JAVA_OPTS. Check your application’s documentation.

Pull the new image and redeploy. Four things catch people out:

Then confirm with java -version that what is running reports a fixed build. After that, remove the flag from every place you set it.

The day after Java4Shell, NGINX released fixes for two memory errors in the way it handles HTTP/2 connections, tracked as CVE-2026-123007 and CVE-2026-123633. A crafted request can crash a worker process, and where an attacker gets past the operating system’s memory protections it can run code inside that worker. Both flaws need HTTP/2 to be enabled on the server, and the same releases fix other NGINX issues that do not depend on HTTP/2, so update either way.

The Docker Hardened Images for NGINX were rebuilt on NGINX 1.30.7 and 1.31.9 and published on November 18, the same day as the upstream release. To patch, pull the image again and redeploy, and move any tag pinned to an older exact version to a fixed one. nginx -v prints the version, and the fixed ones are 1.30.7 and 1.31.9. The notes on pull policy, mirrors and your own images in the section above apply to NGINX images in the same way.

The vulnerable code is in the Java runtime inside the image, and if you are using DHI, shipping the fix becomes our job. Whether that code can be reached in your deployment depends on things an image cannot see: the CPU it is scheduled onto, the options you start it with, the processes it starts, and the traffic you send it.

Track every Java vendor and ship fixed runtimes in DHI as soon as they are released

Track every Java vendor yourself and rebuild your base images on each fixed runtime

Tell you which images are fixed and which are still waiting

Find out from each vendor which of its builds are fixed

Tell you what we know, and update this post

Follow the OpenJDK, Oracle and vendor advisories as they change

Three jobs stay with you whichever image you run.

It was only four weeks ago that the news was PHP2Shell and the eMudhra certificate compromise. Calif, the security research team that found Java4Shell and helped find PHP2Shell, says it used AI to find this flaw in about an hour and that the whole effort cost less than $200 in tokens. When a flaw like this can be found and weaponized at that price, it is reasonable to expect more of them.

If you lead a team or answer for how your organization builds and ships software, the questions after a week like this one are whether you were exposed and how quickly you can prove you were not. It is imperative to have a trusted foundation in place before the next flaw appears, and Docker Hardened Images is that foundation to adopt. This week Docker began publishing fixed Java and NGINX images to the catalog within a day of each disclosure, with the images still waiting listed in the status table above. Each image ships with a signed SBOM and SLSA Build Level 3 provenance, so you have a record of what it contains and how it was built.

The Docker Hardened Images catalog is free and open source under Apache 2.0, and moving one of your services onto a hardened image typically means changing one line in its Dockerfile. If you are not building on Docker Hardened Images yet, start today.

7 Surfaces You Should Never Use Ice Melt On

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Avoid damaging salt-sensitive outdoor surfaces with these expert-backed suggestions.

Ice melt salt is an effective option for preventing and removing ice from sidewalks, driveways and stairs, but it isn’t suitable for every surface. Applying ice melt to certain materials can cause significant damage and should be avoided. Ahead, learn which surfaces are most vulnerable to damage from ice melt, why it happens and what safer alternatives you can use to keep outdoor areas free from ice.

Ice melt salt chemically reacts with certain materials, accelerates freeze-thaw cycles and alters surface moisture levels to cause damage. The type of damage will depend on the surface and how it interacts with ice melt.

Ice melt contains rock salt, calcium chloride and/or magnesium chloride to melt ice faster and at lower temperatures. While all melting salt is corrosive and can damage outdoor surfaces, ice melt products can react differently than rock salt alone.

Ice melt seeps deeply into porous cement-based materials. The calcium chloride in ice melt can react with elements of concrete or brick to form calcium oxychloride crystals. As the crystals grow, they generate extreme pressure that can cause cracks, surface spalling and crumbling.

De-icing products accelerate freeze-thaw cycles in porous cement-based surfaces, causing them to break down. “It makes the freezing and thawing cycle more aggressive, which is what causes the surface to break up,” Murphy says.

Though less damaging than rock salt, ice melt can still contribute to deterioration. Calcium chloride and magnesium chloride attract and retain moisture, increasing the potential for freeze-thaw damage.

Conversely, ice melt draws moisture out of organic materials, causing them to dry out. Over time, this moisture loss can lead to warping, cracking, splitting and other forms of deterioration.

Ice melt accelerates metal oxidation to create rust. It can corrode rebar embedded in concrete, potentially causing cracks and structural damage, as well as rusting exposed metal stairs and railings.

It’s best to avoid applying salt to these salt-sensitive surfaces.

“New concrete is the most vulnerable because it takes weeks to fully cure, so salt on the surface will cause the top few millimeters to flake off,” Murphy says. To be safe, avoid salting new concrete for the first season.

“Cracked concrete opens up for water to get inside, and salt in the water will only make that worse because it will freeze and thaw more aggressively,” Murphy says.

All ice melt products will damage hard-scape surfaces due to chemical reactions and freeze-thaw cycles. “If you are using them, use them sparingly and remove them as soon as possible to decrease continuous exposure for your hardscape areas,” Zurcher says.

Natural stone like limestone and flagstone can react to ice melt compounds. The porous surface is vulnerable to damaging freeze-thaw cycles as well.

Harsh de-icers can damage dyed, painted or stamped concrete surfaces by breaking down color pigments.

Metal stairs and railings will rapidly rust when salt is applied to them, so it’s best to avoid it.

Ice melt draws moisture from wood, causing cracking, warping and raised fibers. It also degrades paint and stain and leaves behind chalky white salt stains.

Sand: Sand doesn’t melt ice, but does provide traction to prevent slips.

Wood ash: Instead of throwing away wood ash, use it as a salt alternative to melt ice and also provide improved traction.

Electric mats: Installed before cold weather sets in, electric melting mats warm up and prevent ice from ever forming. These can be very effective on stairs and sidewalks.

Weed torch: Weed torches are handy when you need to melt a lot of ice quickly. Do not use one to melt ice on wood and avoid painted or stained concrete as well.

“The best way to reduce damage from these chemicals is to shovel snow as soon as possible,” Zurcher says, so you can use less of them. Use ice melt sparingly, and only as directed. A handful is enough to treat three square yards. Instead of using ice melt alone, mix a small amount with sand to treat surfaces.

Regular sealing protects cement-based products.  “Reseal every three to five years to make sure no water can get in, salty or not,” Murphy says. Additionally, use chloride-free de-icing products, or those labelled as “concrete-safe”.

Newly poured concrete is the most vulnerable to salt damage, but all concrete surfaces will experience damage with enough prolonged exposure to salt.

It depends on the extent of the damage. Major cracks and breakage mean concrete needs to be removed and repoured, Zurcher says, while surface damage could be skim-coated, with potentially limited results.

Pet-safe products will have similar effects on hardscape areas, Zurcher says, as they still contain salt.

Space station horror-FPS Derelikt is a lively retro throwback to Alien: Resurrection, though it's better at shooting than scaring

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

I'm a reasonable man. I don't expect every PS1-styled, cosmic horror, nakedly Alien: Resurrection-homaging retro FPS to suffocate me under a blanket of misery. Some of the lighter moments in Derelikt, which released last week, even land: I was grinning my cheeks off as I gorged on the Eldtrich knowledge sealed within an alien obelisk, while repeatedly hanging up on the aghast scientist who kept videocalling me to stop.

Still, Derelikt is fortunate that it's a good shooter, as it often gets muddled on whether it wants to be a horror game as well. It certainly threatens the spooks, trapping you in an oppressively dingy asteroid rig in the orbit of Saturn - the scariest of the ringed gas giants! - and warping most of the other staff into vaguely echinodemic monsterpeople. Also, opening the inventory reveals it to be made of rectangles, a classic symptom of a video game character about to get mangled in the nerves and face.

But nah, shotgun go boom boom. It turns out Derelikt's derelict dwellers are both perfectly susceptible to mundane lead, and sparing users of ambush tactics at most; several even stand around with their backs turned, as if waiting for you to surprise them with a lethal pipe swing. And, even on Medium difficulty, the opening spell of fight-or-run scarcity quickly gives way to an abundance of ammo and health pickups that can easily fill the pocket grid to bursting. I don't claim to have been playing perfectly, but I'm about halfway through and the only way I see myself running low on bullets is if I start using them to punch the complete works of Thomas Campion into a wall.

It's indecisive tonally, as well. The dialogue, and there are a fair few survivors to chat with, seems interested in emulating the gravitas-starved, "Woah man" style of New Millennium action game scripts. Which I can sort of see the logic behind, given the visuals and sound design also, and arguably much more painstakingly, mimic that era. But the downside remains that it ends up with a gravitas-starved, "Woah man" style, just with an extra dash of sideways-winking awareness. Again, it only sometimes works, and it's never scary or eerie. But then there are also more earnest attempts to spin uneasiness, with pages of deranged scribbles left behind by the dwindling crew, and at least a couple of genre-classic wall daubings, which tend to leave me wishing developers Visuwyg had just picked a lane.

Again, though, underneath all the smartarsing is a quality little shooter. The retro presentation does little to soften the heavy, industrial kick of its gunfire, and although many of the meatier freaks are more absorbent than they look, their pained thrashing really helps sell the impact of every individual shot. I also don't really mind the lack of Dead Spacey surprise attacks, as the station's narrow confines tend to force you into tenser, close-range encounters in an organic way anyhow.

More impressive still is how Derelikt manages to fabricate a cold, apprehensive atmosphere befitting its fallen space base, in direct defiance of its own quasi-comedic script. This is mainly thanks to the clanging, low-lit station itself, which teases you with just enough flashes of comfort – a stretch of marble floor here, a messy rec room there – to make your quick return to its steel guts feel all the more unpleasant. Some credit must also go to the puzzling, the cardkey hunts and code searches of which initially had me fearful of the more obstinate, opaque, classic horror style preferred by fellow backwards-looker Silver Pines. Instead, it's more like a compacted Deus Ex, hinting at the need to peek at people's computers and scour the environments for the needed codes. Which makes for generally smoother progress, yes, but also gets you more involved and present inside Derelikt's infested walls.

Midway-through impressions, then: it's a little silly, and perhaps a little confused, but Derelikt finds its focus whenever something needs making dead. I shall continue blasting xenos and burning my eyes out with magic rocks until – no, madam, please hold – further notice.

Google staff push back on cutting off Search for schools that reject AI

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Schools that switch off Google's AI tools will lose Search too, and staff warn that could drive them away.

More than 540 Google employees and contractors have signed a union petition asking the company to let K-12 schools use Search without its AI features, Bloomberg reported. Google plans to turn AI off by default for students, but schools that want Search must accept the AI that comes with it.

More than 540 Google employees and contractors have signed a petition asking the company to let schools use Google Search without its AI features, Bloomberg reported on Thursday. The Alphabet Workers Union, which rallied staff over job security in July, organised the letter.

“Cutting off access to Search for schools that don’t wish to use AI will not help persuade our users,” the workers wrote. They warned that schools could end up “shutting out Google products altogether.”

The letter is addressed to CEO Sundar Pichai, head of Search Elizabeth Reid and education chief Chris Phillips. Google did not immediately comment to Bloomberg.

Earlier this month, Google said it would switch off Gemini and Search for K-12 student accounts unless schools turn them on. But AI is built into Search, so schools that switch Search back on get its AI features too. Schools that want no AI at all must go without Google Search.

The plan drew criticism from parents and schools. Senator Ed Markey wrote to Google on 9 November, asking whether schools could keep Search with the AI switched off. In July, Common Sense Media called Google’s AI Search an “unacceptable risk” for students.

The stakes are high for Google. Its Chrome operating system, which will stop getting updates in 2034, ran on 58% of laptops and tablets shipped to US schools last year, per Futuresource Consulting.

Earlier this month, a family sued Google over its daughter’s conversations with its AI tools on a school device, and Google said it was reviewing the claims. AI companies already face a growing wave of lawsuits over alleged harms to users, including children.

Leaving Google is not easy for schools, said Dan Carroll, co-founder of edtech startup Clever. Chromebooks are built around Google’s software, and switching search engines takes time and training.

“It’s a real strategic pickle,” Carroll said.

Here Are All the Surprise Star Cameos in Taylor Swift’s 2026 CMA Awards Performance

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

From host Lainey Wilson to Sugarland and beyond, country stars took part in Swift's "I Knew It, I Knew You" and "Our Song" set.

Man, it’s been awhile – 13 years to be exact – since Taylor Swift last performed at the CMA Awards, but when she returned to take the stage at the Bridgestone Arena in Nashville on Wednesday (Nov. 18), she did it in style and made sure it sparkled not only thanks to her sequined outfit, but with literal stars – country music stars, that is.

The whimsical performance – a mashup of her Toy Story 5 hit (which has topped the Billboard Hot 100 for two weeks so far) “I Knew It, I Knew You” and “Our Song” (No. 16 peak) from her self-titled 2006 debut album – kicked off backstage in her dressing room. Eagle-eyed Swifties likely noticed that quite a few of her debut-era outfits were strategically placed around the space. But look a little closer, and viewers might see that she had a very small but special – albeit not breathing – guest in the room: Jessie the doll from the Toy Story franchise.

That was just the first of numerous surprise sightings throughout the performance, which included several nods to the film series, including the backup dancers who were an homage to Woody, the blue sky and fluffy white clouds on the front of her guitar that harken back to the wallpaper in Andy’s room, the tree on stage that brought viewers back to Tire Swing Hill, and of course, the 14-time Grammy winner’s glittery, Jessie-inspired cowgirl outfit.

And as Swift left her dressing room and moved through the backstage area as she effortlessly sang “I Knew It, I Knew You,” country greats such as Little Big Town, Brooks & Dunn, host Lainey Wilson, Eric Church and “Whispering Bill” Anderson all greeted her on the way, with Keith Urban waiting on the Toy Story-inspired stage to perform alongside her.

Keep scrolling to see all of the country music stars who joined Taylor Swift for her first CMA Awards performance in more than a decade.

Take a closer look and you’ll see that the Jessie doll from the Toy Story franchise is sitting on the couch between some of the pop superstar’s outfits from her debut era.

Swift kept Paisley busy, as the “Whiskey Lullaby” crooner donned some rubber gloves to polish her CMA Awards trophies.

Cheese! The pop superstar struck a pose with Little Big Town for a quick snap.

Sugarland made a short and sweet appearance.

The singer helped move Brooks & Dunn’s card game along as she made her way through the backstage area during her set.

Swift plopped Kix Brooks’ burgundy cowboy hat on Lainey Wilson’s head, while the CMA Awards host handed the singer a pair of sunglasses.

Swift stopped for a quick and cool fist bump with Eric Church.

“Whispering Bill” was all smiles as he handed the superstar artist her sparkling Toy Story-themed guitar.

Urban was the only country artist to join Swift for the onstage part of her performance.

Russia Opens Legal Bitcoin, Ethereum and USDT Trading to Retail Investors Under New Rules

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Russia finally allowed retail access to Bitcoin, Ethereum, and USDT on regulated exchange operators under its new rules that took effect today, Nov. 17.

Russia has finally opened its crypto market for retail customers as its central bank today enabled trading in Bitcoin, Ethereum, and USDT for the general public. With the law signed by President Vladimir Putin, the Bank of Russia officially rolled out BTC, ETH, and USDT for public trading on domestic exchanges, beginning Nov. 17, 2026.

The new regulation, announced by the Bank of Russia last week, took effect today, Nov. 17. The move allows individual customers to purchase these assets through intermediaries, including brokers, exchanges, and authorized crypto marketplaces. This means ordinary investors can now buy up to 300,000 rubles ($3,700) in a year through a single broker, exchange, or asset manager, after passing a mandatory test.

The announcement expands access to regulated crypto trading in the country and connects the assets with TradFi. This allows non-qualified investors to buy and trade BTC, ETH, and USDT directly through traditional exchanges and clearing systems.

However, qualified investors are not subject to such asset limits and trading restrictions.

The development points out that Russia has taken decisive action to deepen the integration of the crypto market with traditional finance. The debut indicates surging retail interest in cryptocurrency amid regulatory evolution.

It signals a strategic shift in how traditional exchanges handle digital assets. Accommodating retail clients signals growing confidence in the crypto market’s maturity. This highlights rising demand for coordinated exposure to crypto assets within Russia’s capital markets.

The announcement introduces new entry gateways that give retail investors organized access to the crypto market, redefining how these assets are traded locally and internationally.

In the past, crypto in Russia operated in a highly restrictive, complex regulatory environment.  Now, the regulator is moving toward a balance between investor freedom and financial stability. In July, the central bank opened the door to crypto for companies to facilitate global trade. This month, it introduced new legislation that allows retail investors to trade crypto within specified limits.

Roberto De Zerbi Ready to Unleash Mykhailo Mudryk for Tottenham vs Ipswich

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Tottenham Hotspur have had a very mixed start to their Premier League season so far, but they will be desperate to get back on the winning trail against Ipswich Town this weekend.

The north London side saw a run of three consecutive victories brought to an end prior to the international break as they fell to a 1-0 defeat to Leeds at Elland Road, which left them 11th in the table.

But Roberto De Zerbi's side will now have a long stretch of games to put together some impressive performances and pick up some much-needed points to climb the table, and also convince the fans that the Italian is definitely the right man to be in charge of this project after consecutive 17th-placed finishes.

One of the biggest problems that Spurs have had so far in this campaign is scoring goals. The team have managed to score just 10 goals in their 10 Premier League games so far, with summer signing Jan Paul van Hecke the only player to have scored more than once.

That includes central striker Dominic Solanke, who has played in all 10 games but so far managed just one goal, an injury-time equaliser against Man Utd at Old Trafford.

The England international has really struggled to find a place in De Zerbi's system that suits him, with his overall play not good enough to link, and his finishing far from consistent enough to make him being a guaranteed starter make sense.

Considering Tottenham splashed big money on Savio and Omar Marmoush in the summer to bolster the attack too, there is considerable competition for places now. Mohammed Kudus has three assists so far, more than anyone else in the squad, while James Maddison has been a key player, Mathys Tel has impressed, and Dejan Kulusevski has returned from a serious knee injury.

Youngster Luca Williams-Barnett has also impressed in his brief cameos off the bench, and it feels like a matter of time before the manager decides to cut his losses on Solanke as a starter in the number nine role.

Both Tel and Marmoush are capable of playing there, and with summer signing Mykhailo Mudryk expected to be fit to finally be available, De Zerbi is surely now ready to unleash him on the Premier League and get him into the side.

De Zerbi and Mudryk worked together at Shakhtar Donetsk, prior to his move to Chelsea, and the Italian admitted that he felt the Ukraine international has the potential to win the Ballon d'Or in the future. Having that talent available isn't an option from the bench when you need to score more goals, and a start is surely not far away.

The talented winger has featured for just 16 minutes so far this season before he picked up a muscle injury, but he is now believed to be closing in on a return.

Speaking this week, the 25-year-old admitted that he was hungry to get back to his best, having been told by Blues boss Xabi Alonso that he needed to impress out on loan.

"He [Alonso] came up and told me straight to my face, 'You’d better go, play, get some game time, and then come back to Chelsea. I’m counting on you."

Having a player who knows the system, is eager to impress, and has pace to burn sounds like it could be the perfect remedy for Spurs' attacking issues. It also gets Solanke out of the firing line, and the starting lineup, which would be a boost too.

Spurs have got more than enough talent to start scoring goals, and this is a chance after the international break to return to winning ways, and for Mudryk to hit the ground running for his new team, and help his manager get out of a hole.

I Think I Should Have Seen That Bond Casting Coming When Slow Horses Dropped Its Renewal Announcement

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Fans Have BIG Reactions To Robert Pattinson Becoming The New Bond (And Pass The Popcorn)

Now That We Know Who Is Playing James Bond, Who Could Be The Next Bond Girl?

The Full Circle Moment Robert Pattinson (Kinda) Told Us Zendaya Is Responsible For His 007 Role

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Last Monday morning was an exciting time for fans of British spy thrillers. First, Slow Horses was renewed for Seasons 9 and 10, meaning we’ll be getting at least four more seasons of the hit show, available with an Apple TV subscription. Then, just hours later, Amazon MGM finally dropped the bomb we've all been waiting for and announced that the next actor to play James Bond would be Robert Pattinson. Many of us had been waiting for that for months. Years, even, yet it still came as a surprise, given the latest rumors. In hindsight, I probably should have been clued in that the announcement was imminent after the Slow Horses news dropped.

Over the last few weeks and months, there have been a ton of rumors regarding the next Bond ahead of the long-awaited Bond 26 finally beginning production (we’re still not sure when that will actually happen). Names like Jacob Elordi, Henry Cavill, Callum Turner, and more have been rumored. Slow Horses star Jack Lowden was also heavily rumored in recent weeks. Lowden, who plays River Cartwright on the show about disgraced MI5 agents, was looking to make the jump to MI6 and become the ninth man to play Bond.

It’s certainly possible for an actor to appear in multiple projects, but given Slow Horses' aggressive filming schedule (they have already wrapped Season 7, due next year, and are filming Season 8), it might have been a tough schedule to work out for Lowden had he been named Bond. When the next two seasons were announced, it should have clued me in that Lowden likely wasn’t going to be James Bond going forward. It’s quite possible that Apple may have actually been waiting on the Bond decision to announce it was moving forward with more seasons of Slow Horses, too. So, when the news dropped about the show's renewal, I should have known Bond’s announcement was on deck.

Interestingly, despite the announcements coming almost at the same time, there are also rumors that Lowden might not be part of Seasons 9 and 10. A report in Variety calls Lowden’s return “uncertain.” I’m not sure what to make of that. The book-to-screen adaptation has been adapting one book per season, generally. Season 6 was an exception, as it combined two books, the 6th and 7th in Mick Herron’s series Slough House. That's not all, though.

Warning! There are some spoilers ahead from the books Slow Horses is adapted from!

That would presumably mean that Season 7 will be an adaptation of book #8, Bad Actors. As fans of the TV show know, Season 6 ended on a cliffhanger with River having been poisoned by the Russian assassins. Book readers know that River spends Bad Actors on the sidelines, recovering from the assassination attempt, and doesn’t appear in the novel at all. If the show has adapted Bad Actors for Season 7, might Lowden be mostly absent? Cartwright does return in the ninth book, Clown Town, which we do know is going to be Season 8 of the show.

Here’s where it gets interesting. As of today, Clown Town is the most recent book to be published in the Slough House series, so we don’t know what Herron or the producers intend to take the series, so all eyes are on Lowden!

Premier League predictions: Pochettino starts life at Man Utd against rivals Liverpool

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

The Opta supercomputer has delivered its match predictions ahead of the latest weekend of the 2026-27 Premier League season following the latest international break.

During the two-week hiatus, there were two managerial changes, with Mauricio Pochettino replacing Michael Carrick at Manchester United, who sit in the relegation zone, and Thomas Frank succeeding Pierre Sage at Crystal Palace, who prop up the table.

At the other end of the standings, Arsenal hold a five-point lead, having won each of their last five matches.

They sit above high-flying Brighton, while Manchester City slipped down to third following three straight losses.

Matchday 11 sees fixtures take place on Saturday, Sunday and Monday, and ahead of what should be another enticing group of Premier League fixtures, we check out the Opta supercomputer's match predictions.

Manchester City's strong start to the season came crashing down before the break, and they are looking to get back to winning ways when they host Fulham on Saturday.

City have lost three straight league games, as many as in their previous 36 (W25 D8); since the start of 2007-08, they have only lost four in a row twice, doing so in August 2008 and December 2024.

However, City have won their last 17 Premier League games against Fulham and have won their last 20 against the Cottagers in all competitions.

— Manchester City (@ManCity) November 17, 2026

And our predictive model makes them the favourites to triumph at 66.1%, with no team having a higher win probability across the fixtures this weekend. Fulham's hopes of a win sit at 15.5%.

Fulham struggled at the start of the season, but they are unbeaten in their last four league games (W2 D2) and have lost just one of their last seven (W2 D4).

Onto the 15:00 kick-offs, Aston Villa are looking to bounce back after throwing away a late lead in their thrilling 4-4 draw with Man Utd against Sunderland.

Villa have lost just one of their last 12 top-flight matches against Sunderland (W5 D6), going down 3-1 at the Stadium of Light in January 2016.

And Villa are unbeaten in their last six league games (W3 D3), though they dropped points from winning positions in all three draws.

Sunderland, meanwhile, are looking to win successive Premier League games for the first time this season following their 1-0 victory over Chelsea last time out.

It is Villa who are the favourites to triumph, winning 59.3% of the supercomputer's pre-match simulations for Unai Emery's 150th league game in charge, to Sunderland's 19.4%.

A rampant Bournemouth (55.3%) are looking to continue their push up the table against Nottingham Forest (21.6%), who ended their slump against Man City before the break.

The Cherries, who have won each of their last eight matches in all competitions, are unbeaten in all eight of their Premier League games against Forest (W4 D4).

Bournemouth have pushed their way into the top seven after a slow start to the campaign, and they are now aiming to win five straight games in the top-flight for the very first time.

As for Forest, they are winless in six league away games against Bournemouth (D2 L4), though they are looking to win successive top-flight matches for the second time this calendar year (a run of three in April/May).

And a victory would be the perfect result for Oliver Glasner, who is set to take charge of his 100th Premier League match (W33 D32 L34), though he is yet to beat Bournemouth in the competition.

Chelsea went into the international break after a disappointing loss to Sunderland, while Leeds United ended their five-game winless streak with a win over Tottenham.

The Blues are expected to come out on top at Stamford Bridge, with a 51.1% win probability, given they have won 10 points in five top-flight home games this term (W3 D1 L1).

How to Recover From High School

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 19, 2026 | Reviewed by Michelle Quirk

High school may have been a high for many. But if it was a low for you, you ain't alone. A 2025 YouGov survey found that 12 percent of respondents disliked high school while 8 percent hated it. Consider me among the 8 percent.

Having a lot more "grrrrs" than "rah-rahs" in high school could leave you holding onto Rs—namely, resentment and regret—which could keep you trapped in your high school days—and daze—way longer than you'd want. Here's how to instead truly graduate from all that:

A friend once described high school as "The last time that you had no choice of who is going to be around you most of the time," because your high school totally depended on where your family happened to live and what resources and connections they happened to have. Therefore, chances were quite high that you were around high schoolers who didn't fit very well with you.

My high school, for example, had very little diversity and was filled with people not used to dealing with those of different racial, ethnic, cultural, and socioeconomic backgrounds and ways of thinking. For example, my high school soccer team had never seen someone like me play soccer. So, it was pretty common to hear racial stereotypes and epithets hurled around throughout my teenage years, making me feel quite alone then.

This brings up an age-old problem—or perhaps an age-young one. Your high school classmates were in their mid-teens at the time. Their brains and senses of self were still developing like fruitcake batter that had just entered the oven. That, along with their lack of life experience, left them quite limited in their ability to process things around them, know right from wrong, and think for themselves. There are reasons why rental car companies still charge extra until you've gotten past your 25th birthday.

Does that guarantee that all of your high school classmates will eventually grow out of their high school selves? Of course not. Nevertheless, surely you wouldn't base your worth on what any teenager would say about you now? Heck, I wouldn't even listen to my own high school self.

That's because I was still full of "Never Have I Evers" back then—way before I had actually traveled around the world, met a much greater diversity of people, developed real friendships, and engaged in real romantic relationships. For a while, it was hard to look back at my naive high school self without a ton of cringe. But I have since learned to forgive my teenage self—recognizing that I didn't know better at the time.

Part of healing is forgiving your high school classmates as well. For example, the female classmate who once told me, "You are smart and funny, so I like hanging out with you, but you are so incredibly ugly," probably wasn't used to seeing a diverse range of looks and people. Also, her frontal lobe may not yet have been developed enough to tell her, "OK, maybe don't say something like that if you want that person to help you with your homework or teach you how to play sports."

Of course, the fact that to save money my dad—rather than, you know, a real barber—would regularly cut my hair—leaving my head looking like that of a LEGO person—probably didn't help. But eventually you realize that you can recover from not only a bad haircut but also high school. After all, high school is only four years and not forever—lasting about as long as a pair of heavily distressed jeans.

It is important, though, to treat any distressing high school experience like you would a pair of out-of-fashion distressed jeans. Throw them away so that they don't keep taking up space—space that you could otherwise use for new memories, experiences, people, and growth. In fact, get rid of anything that may trigger low high school memories, such as severing ties with anyone who doesn't recognize that you have outgrown your high school years. If you do find it difficult to move on from those years, get help from others, such as a professional therapist.

You'll need to free up that mental and emotional space because you may have a lot of catch-up growing to do. I certainly did, which probably delayed a lot of things and led to a lot of late blooming. But don't despair. Think of going through life as what the character Kandi said on an episode of "Two and a Half Men" about being with Alan: "[It's like being] in the back seat of a car driven by a really smart kangaroo. He may go up on the curb a couple of times, but he'll get you there." The key then is to learn from your faulty high school days—understand what went wrong, what you did wrong, and learn from it. Adapt accordingly.

Now, throwing stuff out doesn't mean simply suppressing your bad high school memories and feelings. That can be like hiding a ferret in your pants. It could end up eating at your rather sensitive spots over time and emerging at the most unexpected, inopportune times. Instead, consider directly confronting your past and processing it.

In the process, you may even find that your memories don't match reality. The supposedly cool things you missed out on may not have been so cool. Those who made you feel bad may have actually felt very bad about themselves.

In lieu of going to my high school reunion, I had dinner with someone I actually liked from high school. We had never really talked about our high school experiences until then. But it soon became apparent that her high school experience was similarly negative. When you are open about such experiences, you may end up realizing that you weren't quite as alone as you thought you were.

Bruce Y. Lee, M.D., M.B.A., is a writer, journalist, professor, systems modeler, computational and digital health expert, physician, avocado-eater, and entrepreneur, not always in that order.

Mortgage Purchase and Refi Activity Hits New Low in Q3

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Data for the third quarter for 2026 continues to showcase the effects of high rates and elevated housing-related costs, as the latest data reveals that the quarterly slowdown was driven by declines in both home purchase and refinance lending.

ATTOM—the California-based property data and real estate analytics company—found that all mortgages and lending in housing fell in Q3 2026, in the latest U.S. Residential Property Mortgage Origination Report.

According to the report, a total of 1.29 million purchase and refinance mortgages secured by residential property (one in four units) originated in the third quarter, which is down 12% from Q2 2026 and down 15% compared to the same period last year.

“The increase in refinance activity we saw late last year and into early this year cooled in the third quarter, with refinancing declining more than purchase lending,” ATTOM CEO Rob Barber said. “Purchase loans also slipped to their lowest third-quarter level since 2012, as higher mortgage rates and elevated average purchase loan amounts continued to strain affordability for many buyers.”

Barber—alongside other economists—attribute the third quarter’s cooling housing activity to the sharp jump in mortgage rates. After the Federal Reserve’s rate hike in September, the average 30-year fixed-rate mortgage jumped to 7.40% in mid-October, and has slowly decreased but remains over 7%.

Purchase loans fell by 9% quarter-to-quarter, for a total of nearly 730,000. The report notes that this drop is the lowest third quarter since 2012. Annually, purchase loans were down 12%, although the average purchase loan increased by 3% to nearly $412,000.

Purchase lending was also down in Q3 2026, totaling $300.6 billion or down 10% from last quarter, and down 10% on a yearly basis.

While activity declined, purchase loans accounted for 56.5% of all purchase and refinance originations, which is up from the 54.5% in Q2 2026 and Q3 2025.

Looking locally, 73.6% of the analyzed metros experienced declines in purchase activity. Some cities with populations over 1 million did see quarterly increases:

But even densely-populated cities were not immune to the effect of elevated mortgage rates, with these metros experiencing the steepest quarterly drops:

Alongside purchase loans, refinance activity fell 16% quarter-to-quarter, totaling 561,290 loans. This marks the lowest quarterly total since Q2 2024, and is down 19% from the same period last year.

The share of refinance originations decreased slightly, from 45.5% in Q2 to 43.5% in Q3 2026. Although not a significant drop in shares, refinance lending declined more drastically than purchase activity by city, falling 89.4% on a quarterly basis.

The cities with the worst drops were mostly in the South and along the West Coast:

Breaking it down by government-backed activity, Federal Housing Administration (FHA) loans accounted for 15.7% of all purchase and refinance originations in the third quarter, down from the previous quarter’s 16.1% and last year’s 17.9%. VA-backed loans were also down, representing 6.9% of originations in Q3 after the previous quarter’s 8.2% and 7.2% the same period last year, or the lowest share since Q2 2024.

Again, the 2026 housing market is shaping up to have a muted end, as pending home sales and mortgage applications have been on a downward trend for the past few months.

The increase in housing construction may spell some hope for housing demand, although experts did express skepticism based on longer-term affordability challenges and economic uncertainty.

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The BPF verifier has historically worked by tracing every possible path of execution through a program, checking, at each instruction, that various safety properties hold. This works, but it can result in an exponential increase in the number of states that need checking in programs with complicated control-flow. Alexei Starovoitov, one of the kernel's core BPF maintainers, has shared a patch set that substantially reworks how the verifier tracks states, in order to reduce the size of that exponential explosion.

Man Of Tomorrow’s Superman Improvements Teased By David Corenswet Amid DCU Concerns

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ryan Northrup is a Senior Staff Writer on ScreenRant's Movie & TV News team, where he has authored nearly 5,000 articles, including features and reviews. A graduate of McMaster University, Ryan began his career in public relations before attending Vancouver Film School. There, he built lasting industry connections and optioned a feature film screenplay.

Expect some Superman upgrades in Man of Tomorrow.

Following his introduction as Superman in James Gunn's movie of the same name last year, David Corenswet is set to return as the DC hero next summer for an anticipated sequel. Man of Tomorrow, which arrives following a shakeup at DC in which Gunn and Peter Safran are to step down from their executive leadership roles, will see Superman teaming up with Nicholas Hoult's Lex Luthor to face a new threat.

As the future of DC remains up in the air, Corenswet dropped by The Rich Eisen Show to discuss his time as Superman and what's to come in Man of Tomorrow. Corenswet comments on what it was like filming the original movie and all the firsts that came with that experience, not just in terms of physically transforming himself, but also in terms of becoming comfortable in the suit. He explains:

I mean, looking back, there were a lot of great challenges that came along with it: gaining the weight for the first time in my life, the wire work, the stunt work was a new thing for me. That was wonderful and challenging. I think back to what Christopher Reeve said about his thoughts going into shooting the original Superman: The Movie which was… Ultimately, it’s just you out there in the tights and nobody’s coming to save you.

And so sometimes the biggest challenge was just standing there for a scene in the tights and the trunks and somehow finding a way to not feel silly and not try too hard to make up for it in some way, not act too tough or shy away from what it is. I think it’s one of the great things about the character is that he’s proud to be the big red and blue and he’ll just stand there in his trunks and maybe you’ll laugh at him, but he’ll be happy to have made you laugh.

While addressing how he maintains his confidence while acting in tights next to his costars, Corenswet touches on Lex's advanced armor suit set to feature in the Superman sequel. The actor then jokingly asks why his character doesn't get his own armored suit:

It was really nice anytime I would walk up to somebody and they’d go like, “Oh my god, it’s Superman.” I go, “Okay, I’m going to take that with me for the next few minutes and hopefully that’ll keep my confidence up just long enough to get through the next scene.” Especially when you’re standing across from Rachel Brosnahan, who’s dressed all cool, and she looks so beautiful, and she’s so sharp, and Nicholas Hoult, and his cool tailored suits, and then his war suit in this movie we just shot. He’s got his big green war suit. Like, why can’t I get some armor?

Though Corenswet is careful not to reveal too much about Man of Tomorrow, he does tease that the film features some exciting flying and fight sequences. Speaking seemingly of the stunt and visual effects teams, Corenswet says that "they really up their game" from the first movie. Check out his comment below:

Love the interview scene with Rachel. That was such a fun scene to shoot. It was the first scene that we shot. It was two days and it was like shooting like a 1950s Cary Grant movie. Just the dialogue, quippy, and funny, and just about these characters. There were so many great action pieces. Actually, in this movie, in the next Superman movie that we finished this summer, there are some incredible new flying and fights. They really up their game there. So, in some ways, those are the ones I’m really excited for people to see those, ’cause those were a lot of fun to shoot and I think will end up being a lot of fun to do.

In addition to Corenswet, Brosnahan, and Hoult, Man of Tomorrow is set to feature the return of Skyler Gisondo as Jimmy Olsen, Wendell Pierce as Perry White, Isabela Merced as Hawkgirl, Frank Grillo as Rick Flag Sr., Edi Gathegi as Mr. Terrific, Mikaela Hoover as Cat Grant, and María Gabriela de Faría as the Engineer, among others.

The Superman follow-up will also feature a host of newcomers, including Lars Eidinger as Brainiac, Aaron Pierre as Green Lantern, Milly Alcock as Supergirl, Xolo Maridueña as Blue Beetle, and Matthew Lillard in an undisclosed role. Man of Tomorrow's upgraded flying and fighting scenes, then, will clearly accompany a much more expansive and star-studded cast.

Of course, the recent leadership shakeup at DC Studios has cast a shadow over Man of Tomorrow and has raised questions about the current iteration of DC heroes. After becoming co-CEOs of DC Studios in 2022, Gunn and Safran are confirmed to be stepping down from their roles effective December 1, 2026.

The development follows Paramount Skydance's acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery on October 6, with the resulting company simply called Skydance. Jeremy Latcham is stepping in as president and chief creative officer, replacing Gunn and Safran.

Man of Tomorrow will follow a mixed series of releases for DC. Superman was a modest hit, grossing $618 million and earning strong reviews. Supergirl (2026), however, was a box office bomb this summer. Most recently, Clayface, which hit theaters on October 23, earned positive reviews and became a modest box office success. Gunn and Safran were also broadly successful on the TV front, with Peacemaker, Creature Commandos, and Lanterns all met with warm receptions.

Though many questions remain about Man of Tomorrow and the future of DC more broadly, audiences can evidently expect some exciting upgrades to Superman's flying and fighting scenes. With the sequel potentially serving as Corenswet's last outing as the DC hero, the movie is certainly shaping up to end things on a high note.

United Airlines Cancels All Flights To 7 Destinations: See Full List Now

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

United Airlines, a founding member of Star Alliance, has been continuously reviewing its route network. Just recently, Simple Flying reported that the airline had removed several routes from its third-busiest hub.

Looking at its broader network, however, it has also removed all flights to seven destinations from its summer 2027 schedule compared to this summer. Five of these are domestic destinations, including two airports in Michigan, while the remaining two are international.

According to scheduling data from aviation analytics company Cirium, United has removed all flights to five domestic destinations. These are Nantucket Memorial Airport (ACK) in Massachusetts, Yellowstone Airport (WYS) in Montana, Rhinelander–Oneida County Airport (RHI) in Wisconsin, Pellston Regional Airport (PLN), and Chippewa County International Airport (CIU) in Michigan.

Internationally, the airline has also pulled out of Tulum International Airport (TQO) in Mexico and Blaise Diagne International Airport (DSS) in Dakar, Senegal. All of these services, except Tulum, ended in recent months, as shown in the table below. The Star Alliance member’s last scheduled flight to Tulum is due to operate in January 2027.

None of these destinations currently has United flights scheduled for summer 2027. With that being said, the airline could still bring back some of these flights (such as its seasonal service to Rhinelander) for next summer. However, with the rest of its summer 2027 schedule already on sale, there is very little time for it to add these flights back.

United will operate its final flight to Tulum from Houston's George Bush Intercontinental Airport (IAH) in January 2027. This will mark its complete withdrawal from the Mexican Riviera Maya destination. The airline began serving Tulum in April 2024, just a few months after the airport opened for commercial flights in December 2023.

Following its opening, several airlines were quick to introduce flights, including United, which initially served this Mexican gateway from four hubs: Chicago O'Hare International Airport (ORD), Los Angeles International Airport (LAX), Newark Liberty International Airport (EWR), and Houston. However, the Star Alliance member only operated its Los Angeles service for one season and dropped Chicago last year.

It is now scheduled to end its Newark flights on January 2, followed by Houston two days later. This comes because demand for flights to Tulum has been lower than initially expected. According to US Department of Transportation (DOT) T-100 data, United's flights to the Mexican resort city recorded average load factors ranging from 60 to around 70%.

Other airlines have also reduced their services. JetBlue, for instance, which launched daily nonstop flights from New York's John F. Kennedy International Airport (JFK) in 2024, has since discontinued the route. American Airlines and Delta Air Lines continue to serve Tulum, although the overall number of seats available on flights from the US has fallen considerably, as noted above in the table.

All these places have stopped seeing the carrier, and nearly all in September and October.

Furthermore, United's Dakar service also seems to have suffered from weak demand. The airline only began serving Senegal in May 2025, which marked its entry into the West African country. It initially offered three weekly year-round flights from Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD), using its Boeing 767-300ER aircraft.

However, the Chicago-based airline decided to discontinue the service less than a year after its launch. According to US DOT data, the route recorded an average monthly load factor of just 58% across the nine months. In fact, during two of those months, the airline filled fewer than 40% of its seats.

With United's departure, Delta will be the only airline offering nonstop flights between the US and Dakar. The SkyTeam founding member operates five weekly flights from New York JFK using its 767-300ER, although this drops to three weekly during the summer season.

GTA 6’s Best-Kept Secret Is the Most Important Part of the Game

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The prologue of GTA 6 starts with a shock

Considering how much of Grand Theft Auto 6 leaked before its launch, you’d think that Rockstar’s latest wouldn’t have anything left to hide. I mean, we’ve really seen it all. But as players immediately discovered when starting their save file, Rockstar (kind of) managed to keep one secret — and it was a huge one.

[Ed. note: This article contains spoilers for the prologue of GTA 6.]

GTA 6 kicks off with a short prologue chapter that sets a few things in motion. First, we get to see the moment Jason and Lucia met, as they both got crossed trying to jack the same car. A rom-com montage ensues, but things take a turn fast. We fast-forward to Lucia sitting in a restaurant as she prepares to pull off a robbery across the street. After nervously stalling​​​​​​​, she works up the courage to stick the place up. Unfortunately, it doesn’t go well. The cops quickly intervene and arrest her.

The twist comes as she’s being put into the back of a cop car. A panicked Lucia begs the cops to take her back to the restaurant where she was holed up before the robbery, because she left something there. The cops discover something that the camera was carefully hiding earlier in the sequence: Lucia’s infant son, Tommy, who she left unattended.

As we soon learn, Tommy is Jason and Lucia’s son — unbeknownst to Jason, until Lucia tells him upon getting out of prison. Tommy now lives with a separate guardian after getting taken from Lucia’s custody; Jason and Lucia only get to see him through occasional photos that get texted to them. It turns out that Jason and Lucia’s whole motivation for making cash in GTA 6 is to fund a family savings account in hopes that they can reunite with their son and give him a normal life.

It’s a shocking revelation that Rockstar desperately wanted to keep hidden as long as possible. No pre-release trailers for the game so much as teased that there was a kid in the picture. When I spoke to Rockstar North co-head Rob Nelson just ahead of the game’s launch, he seemed relieved that the detail had mostly gone unspoiled, despite a cavalcade of leaks. (It actually did leak about a year ago, but it faded into a forgotten rumor in a sea of them by the time GTA 6 actually launched.) For Nelson, that initial shock is an important part of an experience that’s designed to challenge what you think you know about its antiheroes at every turn.

With Tommy comes a mess of complicated character dynamics and anxieties that you’ll see throughout GTA 6’s story. While Lucia seems like an impulsive loudmouth who doesn’t have a care in the world, it becomes increasingly clear that she’s harboring serious insecurity after having Tommy taken from her. She quietly worries that she doesn’t deserve to be a parent after leaving her kid to go pull off a stick-up. While Jason wasn’t part of that, he holds the same fear that someone like him isn’t responsible enough to be a father. Those feelings bleed out whenever the two receive a text with a photo of Tommy and are reminded of the kid who is growing up somewhere without them.

Tommy also adds some welcome tension into Jason and Lucia’s love story, considering that the romance can be largely free of friction otherwise (depending on how you engage with the romance system). At one point, the two broke into a spontaneous conversation about their family. Jason noted that Lucia’s mother doesn’t seem to like him very much, which prompted Lucia to reveal that she lied to her mom and told her that Jason didn’t want to be involved in Tommy’s life. Though that isn’t exactly true given that Jason didn’t know his son existed, Lucia then recalled a conversation where Jason told her he didn’t want to have kids. That exchange gives us context that explains why Lucia didn’t tell Jason about Tommy upfront. For Nelson, those little details help build a nuanced story of two reluctant parents trying to figure out how to adapt to an unexpected responsibility.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

And while stories about parenting might feel a little played out in a world where the “dad game” has become a tired trope, it’s refreshing to have one that has so much compassion for everyone involved. It’s sympathetic to Lucia’s circumstances, making a point in a flashback to show her struggling to live as a single mother in a world that is hostile to her specific needs. It is careful about letting Jason face some very human insecurities without casting him as a hothead who can only deal in tough love. GTA 6 tells us that two people can survive a bad situation if they’re driven by a love that provides stability in uncertain times.

As players are bound to experience for themselves, Tommy changes so much about the psychology of GTA, even from off-screen. Maybe he won’t make you think twice about robbing a bank, but hopefully you’ll feel like all that crime isn’t for nothing every time you send a few bucks into your savings account.

Rockstar’s magnum opus searches for love and catharsis in desperate times — and finds them

Why Rockstar Games Includes Bigfoot And UFOs To Find In Its Big Open-World Games: ‘We Love That Stuff’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BigfootGrand Theft Auto 6GTA 6Rob NelsonRockstar Games

For nearly as long as Rockstar has been making big open-world games, like the recently released Grand Theft Auto 6 and 2018’s Red Dead Redemption 2, players have been exploring these worlds for strange secrets, supernatural events, and cryptids like Bigfoot and Mothman. And sometimes they actually find things along these lines, which made me curious as to why Rockstar includes these oddities.

I spoke to Rob Nelson, Rockstar North’s head of production and the de facto boss on GTA 6, ahead of the massive game’s launch. And during our interview, I asked him why so many of its open-world games contain otherworldly or hard-to-explain mysteries and creatures. According to him, there are a few reasons why Rockstar added things like the Skunk Ape, which players have already found lurking in the swamps of Leonida, to GTA 6. For one thing, they have the “team size” to be able to work on all of this extra content in a way they couldn’t long ago. The other reason: “We love it.”

“Also, the fact that Florida is so fucking weird and crazy, we love that stuff,” said Nelson. “We love sort of playing with players. You never know whether it’s real paranormal stuff or it’s phony paranormal stuff. Is it really Bigfoot? Is it a guy wearing a Bigfoot mask? We just love it.”

Nelson explained that the team also wanted the map in GTA 6 to feel completely full and packed with things to do and see. They wanted it to be “constantly surprising you.” It also helps that, according to Nelson, the team working on GTA 6 has been together for “such a long time” and that there are entire groups within the studio made up of people who in the past were responsible for a lot more. Now, they can focus on smaller details and specific bits of each game, like adding ghosts in GTA V or a vampire in Red Dead Redemption 2.Â

“They’re dedicated to making these games amazing things,” said Nelson in a way that really made it seem like he was proud of his team.Â

Another reason GTA 6 has so much weird and strange stuff in it is because of how much wilderness is in the game. And Nelson and his team wanted to fill these parts of the map with just as much to do and see as the urban areas. ”

“[We] always want it to feel like there’s something weird out there,” said Nelson. “And hopefully in GTA 6 there will be stuff for people to be finding for a long time to come.â

Arthur Hayes predicts 97% crypto rally weeks after he exited trades

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

His targets also include $2,800 for Ethereum. Hayes gave no deadline and called the setup low conviction.

Arthur Hayes has just set bullish price targets for Ethereum, Zcash, NEAR and Venice Token, less than a month after saying he closed Maelstrom’s directional trading book. His $1,800 Zcash target was 97.3% above the token’s Nov. 18.

In a Nov. 17 post, Hayes called the tokens his “Four Horsemen.” He put ETH at $2,800, ZEC at $1,800, NEAR at $6 and VVV at $40. He described the prospective trade size as “small to medium,” expressed little conviction and expected a rally before the end of what he called the AI bubble.

The post gave no deadline for the targets. Hayes gave no details about purchases by him or Maelstrom, or about the fund’s current exposure.

On Oct. 24, CryptoSlate reported that Hayes said he had closed Maelstrom’s directional book and taken profits on ETH, Ethena, Uniswap and other tokens. The exit covered bets on which way prices would move. ETH was among the tokens he sold and one of the four he expects to rally.

Hayes set out his condition for staying on the sidelines in a Nov. 5 essay. He wrote that Maelstrom would wait unless the fiat liquidity picture changed dramatically. His concern was that a decline in AI stocks could make lenders demand higher yields from those companies. That would make debt harder to issue and, in his view, leave less dollar liquidity available to markets.

NEAR and ZEC were names Hayes had publicly walked away from before. In a June 8 essay, he wrote that he had sold NEAR the previous week and gave a separate reason for exiting ZEC, citing a bug in Zcash’s Orchard privacy pool. The two tokens are now back in his public outlook, and ZEC has the widest gap to its target.

Could Earth have been collecting dark matter for billions of years?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Heat measurements from dead stars helped physicists narrow down how dark matter might interact with ordinary matter.

Physicists have spent decades building dark matter detectors deep underground, and they still haven’t caught a single particle.

A new study suggests that Earth itself, along with the Sun, Jupiter and a handful of dead stars, might trap certain kinds of dark matter better than any lab instrument.

These bodies could have been collecting slow-moving particles for billions of years.

The researchers used neutrino data and heat measurements to rule out some combinations of particle mass and interaction strength that laboratory experiments couldn’t probe.

The work was led by Alejandro Ibarra from the Technical University of Munich, Merlin Reichard from the Korea Institute for Advanced Study and Gaurav Tomar from the Sogang University in Seoul.

About 85% of the matter in the universe is dark matter. We know it’s there because galaxies spin faster than their visible matter can explain and light bends more than it should as it passes them.

Beyond that, very little is known about it. Physicists are stuck in a frustrating loop.

You can’t identify something you can’t see, and you can’t design a way to see it without some idea of what it is. So they make a guess, build an experiment to test it, and so far the answer has always been no.

One guess is that dark matter isn’t completely dark. Its particles might respond to electricity and magnetism very faintly, carrying a tiny fraction of an electron’s charge or acting like microscopic magnets.

The paper describes five such properties. One of them, the “anapole moment,” is a doughnut-shaped kind of magnetism.

Any of the five would let a dark matter particle occasionally bump into an atom.

Underground detectors wait for those bumps. A slow particle taps an atom so gently that the instrument doesn’t notice.

In practice, these detectors are more sensitive to faster dark matter particles.

Planets and stars work the other way around. A fast particle shoots straight through them. A slow one only needs to lose a little speed in a collision before gravity grabs it and won’t let go.

Over billions of years, trapped particles would sink toward the center and crowd together. Once packed tightly enough, they could meet and annihilate, turning into other particles.

Some of those would be neutrinos, which pass through rock almost as if it weren’t there. The rest would deposit their energy as heat.

“In this work, we considered four celestial bodies: Earth, the Sun, Jupiter and white dwarfs,” Tomar said.

“There are dedicated experiments, for example IceCube at the South Pole, looking for neutrinos from the Sun and Earth. For Jupiter and white dwarfs, we considered the heating possibilities.”

For the Sun and Earth, the team used limits from IceCube, a detector built into the Antarctic ice, and from its smaller companion detector, DeepCore.

That included ten years of IceCube data on neutrinos coming from Earth’s core. There was no excess.

Neither Jupiter nor the white dwarfs generate heat through nuclear fusion.

The Cassini spacecraft measured how much heat Jupiter leaks from its interior.

The white dwarfs are burnt-out stellar cores in M4, a cluster of ancient stars. Each one holds about 1.4 times the Sun’s mass in a ball with a radius of roughly 1,250 kilometers.

If a candidate type of dark matter would make these objects give off more heat than they actually do, it can be crossed off.

Earth turned out to be the best test for dark matter with a tiny electric charge. For particles heavier than about ten protons, it set the strongest limits yet.

There are two reasons. First, a charged particle’s pull reaches a long way, so it is easily drawn in. Second, Earth’s core is full of iron, whose atoms each carry 26 protons, which makes collisions more likely.

The white dwarfs set leading limits on two of the more exotic properties, the charge radius and the anapole moment, across a wide range of particle masses.

For very heavy particles, they also beat earlier limits on dark matter’s magnetic dipole moment.

The anapole limits matter for one popular candidate whose only possible electromagnetic property is the anapole moment.

For that candidate, the white dwarfs reach places that particle colliders and underground detectors struggle to get to.

Jupiter was a letdown. In general, it didn’t improve on limits that already existed. None of the four bodies beat earlier limits on the electric dipole moment either.

Underground labs sit beneath a kilometer or more of rock to block cosmic rays. If dark matter interacts strongly with ordinary matter, that same rock could slow it down or stop it before it reaches the detector.

The shield could be hiding the very particles the experiment is looking for.

Planets and stars don’t have this problem. The more strongly dark matter interacts, the better they catch it.

There is one important caveat. The more dark matter surrounds a star, the more of it the star can capture, and nobody has measured how much dark matter M4 contains.

The team had to rely on estimates from models.

“We do not know the dark matter density there. We used estimated values, so if those values change, our limits could become weaker or stronger,” Tomar noted.

Tomar suggests two places to look next. One is even colder white dwarfs in regions with more dark matter, where any extra heat would be easier to detect.

The other is beyond the Solar System, where exoplanets could serve as dark matter traps too.

The study is published in the Journal of Cosmology and Astroparticle Physics.
